# C102: an affine checkpoint law, its true radius, and a scoped domination theorem

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT. Worker max3, allocation round2
under master guidance2765a48d4ff7259e. Completed baseline31; previous
completed32; this return completes33, the completed ceiling. Attempted
ceiling34 includes the historical interruption. No formal gate is allocated.

The original target remains the unrestricted all-nonnegative-word,
all-sufficiently-large-horizon profile W_n(h)>=(1-o(1))/B_n, with W=2R.
It is OPEN. All new statements in this document are one joint candidate;
none is an accepted fact. C101/002d8b071467304a, C99, C100 and other shared
drafts are context, not premises. Every needed new geometric and scalar
estimate is proved below within this joint draft.

Accepted premises:1252b8823bb5a290 for the original definition of W_n and
the directed smooth interpolation residuals; b98bb807d514e80d for the
complete Huber/single-cut functional K_n and original benchmark recurrences;
1fca3e5dd555cb4a for the complete alternating-ray functional F_ray_n;
dfde2fa326c908bf for the Huber-augmented complete universal-lens functional.
No newly found external theorem is used as a proof premise.

## 1. Family and conclusions

Split the ORIGINAL physical word chronologically as

    h=(block_0,b,block_1,d,block_2),
    S_j=sum block_j, j=0,1,2,   b>1, d>1.

Each block is any finite list of nonnegative physical steps, possibly
empty. Their queries, the two checkpoint queries, and the actual terminal
sample are all retained. The horizon n is their original total length.

The following scalar abbreviations are LOCAL to this draft, not changes
to the project glossary or to B_n:

    T=1+S_0, M=1+S_1, N=1+S_2, sigma=1+2S_2,
    A=b-1, E=d-1,
    0<=zeta<E, D=M+zeta, B=E-zeta,
    u>=0, v>0, ell>0, kappa>=0,
    delta=A-Du, Z=Bv-N ell, Q=u^2+v^2.

Let e_1,e_2 be the standard orthonormal basis of the real plane. Define

    g_A=e_1, g_B=-u e_1-v e_2, g_C=ell e_2,
    J102(y)=max{0,<g_A,y>,<g_B,y>-kappa,<g_C,y>},
    F102(x)=min_y {J102(y)+||x-y||^2/2},
    X_0=(T+delta)e_1-Dv e_2.                         (1)

Thus the middle affine intercept is independent of the three gradients.
The declared family includes ALL these parameters satisfying

    delta>=0,
    Dv^2 <= (1+u)delta+kappa,
    0<=kappa<=zeta Q,
    ell Z >= Bu.                                    (2)

The intended gradient is g_A on block_0 and its following checkpoint,
g_B on block_1 and its following checkpoint, and g_C on block_2,
including the final query. Conditions (2) are necessary and sufficient
for exactly this law and these point formulas on the single function (1).

The actual minimizer set, exact true squared radius, and normalized payoff
are respectively

    K102={x_1<=0, x_2<=0, -u x_1-v x_2<=kappa},
    R102^2=(T+delta)^2+(Dv-kappa/v)_+^2,
    V102=[2 ell Bv-sigma ell^2]/R102^2,               (3)

where a_+=max(a,0). The three endpoint value reserves F102-||g||^2/2 are

    delta, zeta Q-kappa, ell Z.                      (4)

They can all be strictly positive with kappa>0. The old homogeneous
initial norm is NOT the correct radius when kappa>0.

Here is the new word-conditioned domination theorem. Suppose the original
block masses and the two actual checkpoint steps satisfy

    A E (T+A) <= M T^2.                              (5)

For EVERY member of (1)--(2), change the parameters to

    zeta'=kappa'=0, u'=u, v'=v, ell'=(E/B)ell.        (6)

Keep the original word and the same block partition. Formula (1) with
these new parameters gives a legal homogeneous law on that WHOLE word,
and its exact true-radius payoff is at least V102. Both trajectories use
one static function of their own; this is a comparison between complete
instances, not a mid-run function replacement.

If i<j are the checkpoint indices, put P=S_0, S=S_1, U=S_2 and define

    Cut_j=E^2/[(T+A+M)^2(1+2U)],
    Pair_ij=A E/(T M).

Consequently, under (5), the ENTIRE affine family obeys

    V102 <= max{Cut_j, Pair_ij^2/(1+2U)}.             (7)

For the ORIGINAL enclosing-m words, define

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_m=1-1/m,
    c_m=lambda(1+1/m), a_m=beta_m/c_m,
    H_0=(), H_(k+1)=(H_k,1+a_m c_m^k,H_k),
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, M_k=1+sum H_k.

The same a_m,c_m occur in every child of this recursion. For m>=128,
(5) holds for EVERY checkpoint pair in this original word. With
gamma102=5949/6088<1, the full conclusions are

    sup_(all C102 parameters and all pairs on H_m) V102
        <= gamma102/(2M_m-1)
         = gamma102 K_n(H_m)
         = gamma102 F_ray_n(H_m),                    (8)
    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) sup V102
        <= 2gamma102/exp(1)<1.                       (9)

In particular an independent middle affine intercept, even with its
genuine nearest-minimizer gain and strictly positive reserves, does not
repair this radial-middle, orthogonal-endpoint two-checkpoint family.
Neither (5) nor the family's geometric restrictions may be discarded.

## 2. Static regularity and the actual minimum

The finite maximum J102 is convex, continuous and nonnegative. For each
x the minimization in (1) is coercive and strictly convex, so it has a
unique minimizer y(x). Write g(x)=x-y(x). Its minimizing condition is

    g(x) in partial J102(y(x)).                      (10)

For completeness, necessity follows by comparing y(x) with
y(x)+t(z-y(x)), using convexity of J102, dividing by t>0, and letting
t decrease to zero. This gives
J102(z)>=J102(y(x))+<x-y(x),z-y(x)> for every z. Conversely that inequality
and expansion of the squared norm show that the objective at z exceeds
its value at y(x) by at least ||z-y(x)||^2/2.

At x,x' add the two subgradient inequalities in (10). With y=y(x),
y'=y(x'), g=g(x), g'=g(x'), the result is

    <g-g',y-y'>>=0,
    ||g-g'||^2 <= <g-g',x-x'>.

Thus g is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the objective at the fixed old minimizer
y and using (10) for the new minimizer yields

    0<=F102(x')-F102(x)-<g,x'-x><=||x'-x||^2/2.

The lower inequality follows more precisely with remainder
||g'-g||^2/2. Hence F102 is differentiable with gradient g. It is convex:
the same lower inequality supplies a supporting affine function at every
x. Thus it is a globally defined convex1-smooth function.

The minimum value is0. Indeed J102 is zero exactly on K102 from (3), and
these points give zero in the minimization. If F102(x)=0, its attained
two nonnegative summands must both vanish; hence y(x)=x belongs to K102.
This proves argmin F102=K102, not merely that0 is an included minimizer.

For u>0, K102 is the closed triangle with vertices
(0,0),(-kappa/u,0),(0,-kappa/v), degenerating to{0} when kappa=0.
For u=0 it is the strip {x_1<=0,-kappa/v<=x_2<=0}.
Every member has x_1<=0 and -kappa/v<=x_2<=0. The point X_0 has positive
first coordinate T+delta and negative second coordinate -Dv. Therefore
its unique closest point in K102 is

    x_star=(0,-min{Dv,kappa/v}).                     (11)

This point belongs to the vertical edge of K102 and separately minimizes
each squared coordinate difference over the two necessary coordinate
intervals. No other point can improve either contribution. Formula (11)
proves R102^2 in (3), including both radius branches and u=0.

## 3. All physical queries and every directed interpolation row

Assign affine costs k_A=k_C=k_star=0 and k_B=kappa. If at a sample x_i
the plane with slope g_i is active at y_i=x_i-g_i, then (10) proves the
claimed gradient and gives

    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2-k_i.

For ANY two such samples, direct expansion of the residual in accepted
1252b8823bb5a290 yields

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>-k_i+k_j <=0.                 (12)

The inequality says exactly that j's active plane dominates i's plane
at y_j. Thus checking activity at all actual queries proves all original
directed rows in both orientations, including every remote cross-block
row. With g_star=0, f_star=0 and y_star=x_star from (11), the same formula
includes both optimum rows. The zero plane is active at every minimizer.

Here are the activities, without dropping any in-block point. At
cumulative physical mass t in block_0, 0<=t<=S_0, the actual proposed
point and proximal point are

    x=(T+delta-t)e_1-Dv e_2,
    y=(delta+S_0-t)e_1-Dv e_2.

At the endpoint y=delta e_1-Dv e_2 the g_A-plane has height delta,
the zero plane0, the g_B-plane -u delta+Dv^2-kappa, and the g_C-plane
-ell Dv. Exactly the first two inequalities of (2) make g_A active.
Earlier points add a nonnegative multiple of e_1. This raises the
g_A-plane relative to every other plane, since its directional slope1
exceeds the other slopes0,-u,0.

The checkpoint b sends the endpoint x to Dg_B. At cumulative mass t
in block_1 the point is (D-t)g_B and its proximal point is

    y=(S_1+zeta-t)g_B.

At the endpoint the g_B-plane height is zeta Q-kappa. It dominates
zero exactly when kappa<=zeta Q, and then it also dominates the g_A-
and g_C-plane heights -zeta u and -zeta ell v. Earlier points have a
larger nonnegative coefficient of g_B, so these comparisons strengthen.

The checkpoint d now sends the actual point to

    x=B(u e_1+v e_2).

At cumulative mass t in block_2 the proximal point for g_C is

    y=Bu e_1+[Bv-(1+t)ell]e_2.

At t=S_2 its g_C-plane height is ell Z. The g_A-plane is Bu, the
g_B-plane is -Bu^2-vZ-kappa, and the zero plane is0. The last inequality
of (2) implies Z>=0 and dominates all three other planes. Earlier points
add a nonnegative multiple of e_2, improving every comparison.

This proves all desired gradients, the original GD updates, and (12) at
every original query. Zero steps merely repeat points. Arbitrarily large
ordinary steps cause no deletion: their partial masses still belong to
the specified intervals.

For necessity, each g_A,g_B,g_C is an exposed vertex of the convex hull
of the four slopes. They are exposed respectively by e_1,-e_2,e_2.
For a finite affine maximum, a subgradient is a convex combination of
the active slopes: otherwise a separating direction contradicts the
directional derivative, which is the largest scalar product with an
active slope. Hence if (10) has one of these exposed slopes, that slope's
own plane must be active. The endpoint comparisons against zero and
the displayed competing planes force precisely (2).

The terminal point is Bu e_1+(Bv-S_2 ell)e_2. Its active-plane formula
gives F102(x_n)=ell Bv-(S_2+1/2)ell^2. This proves V102 and all reserves
in (3)--(4). The numerator is positive: ell Z>=0 implies ell<=Bv/N,
and then 2Bv-sigma ell>=Bv/N>0.

## 4. A concrete whole-word selection with positive intercept and reserves

For every pair with b,d>1 and its ORIGINAL block sums, use

    zeta=E/2, B=E/2, D=M+zeta,
    v=sqrt(A/(4D)),
    u=min{A/(4D), Bv^2/(8N)},
    ell=Bv/(2N), kappa=zeta(u^2+v^2)/2.             (13)

All six parameters are positive. One has delta>=3A/4 and Dv^2=A/4,
so the first endpoint inequality holds strictly even before adding
kappa. The middle reserve is zeta Q/2>0. Finally Z=Bv/2 and

    ell Z=B^2v^2/(4N)>=2Bu>0.

This verifies the entire law by the preceding all-query proof.

A concrete partition rule is to evaluate (3) with (13) on every pair
1<=i<j<=n-2 with h_i,h_j>1, select the largest score, and break ties
lexicographically. This is a finite mathematical selection among
explicit real formulas, not a restatement of Gram feasibility or a
claim that a favorable partition must exist. If there are no such pairs,
this particular rule is unused; the accepted Huber witness is separately
available. The obstruction concerns the C102 family, not the maximum
of that family with a fallback witness.

On every original H_m with m>=3 one may instead select the explicit
pair i=1,j=n-2. Its data are

    b=d=1+beta_m, S_0=S_2=1+a_m,
    S_1=M_m-1-2(1+a_m)-2(1+beta_m).

The last expression is the actual middle subword mass and is
nonnegative. Formula (13) thus gives actual data for the whole original
word without any partition-existence assumption.

At the first shortest physical step h_0=1+a_m, the two queried gradients
are both g_A, with inner product1>0. This explicitly leaves C100's
nonpositive-adjacent-queried-correlation hypothesis. Moreover

    [F102(X_0)+||g_A||^2/2]/||g_A||^2
        =T+delta>h_0,

so this short query leaves C99's short-step value hypothesis as well.
The kappa>0 law also leaves C101's homogeneous-plane hypothesis and
moves the actual minimizer set. These are scope escapes on legal data,
not claims that escaping an old hypothesis suffices for the target.

Translate by (11), divide spatial coordinates by R102 and the objective
by R102^2, and multiply the objective by any L>0. This gives the original
L-smooth convention with true initial distance1, the same normalized
payoff, and the identical physical steps. A point mass on this instance
is already a law. Random rotations or mixtures of individually normalized
instances cannot exceed a bound valid for each realization.

## 5. Exact paid continuation on the same function

Fix the same function and all its old jets. Append any ordinary word of
total nonnegative mass t_extra and require every new queried gradient,
including the new terminal one, to remain g_C. This is possible exactly
when

    0<=t_extra<=Tcarry102,
    Tcarry102=(ell Z-Bu)/ell^2.                     (14)

Indeed the new proximal points are

    Bu e_1+(Z-t ell)e_2, 0<=t<=t_extra.

The new g_C-plane dominates the old g_A-plane precisely when
ell Z-t ell^2>=Bu. This makes Z-t ell nonnegative and therefore also
dominates zero and the g_B-plane, whose height is
-Bu^2-v(Z-t ell)-kappa<=0. Equation (12) proves all old/new and new/new
directed rows. If the inequality fails, the old g_A-plane defeats g_C,
so the requested holding gradient is not the actual gradient.

The function, minimizer set and true initial radius remain fixed. The
exact new payoff is

    V102(t_extra)=V102-2t_extra ell^2/R102^2.        (15)

This is a paid finite continuation. It supplies no free reset and no
unproved scalable succession of changed-gradient checkpoints.

## 6. Affine-intercept domination, including the true-radius gain

Assume the actual word condition (5). The legality conditions imply

    Mv^2 <= (1+u)(A-Mu)-zeta u
           <= (1+u)(A-Mu),                         (16)

by using kappa<=zeta(u^2+v^2) in the first-block inequality and subtracting
zeta v^2. Thus the homogeneous comparison (6) satisfies its first-block
condition. Its middle endpoint has zero reserve and is legal. Its last
condition follows from

    ell'[Ev-Nell']
       =(E/B)^2 ell[Bv-Nell]
       >=(E/B)^2 Bu >=Eu.                          (17)

The preceding static proof applies with zeta=kappa=0, so this comparison
is an actual complete trajectory, not a relaxed feasible parameter list.
Its true squared radius is

    R_hom^2=(T+A-Mu)^2+M^2v^2.                     (18)

To compare true radii, kappa<=zeta Q gives

    R102^2 >= [T+A-(M+zeta)u]^2
                +(Mv-zeta u^2/v)_+^2.             (19)

For 0<=t<=zeta define the entirely explicit scalars

    x(t)=T+A-(M+t)u,
    y(t)=Mv-t u^2/v,
    H(t)^2=x(t)^2+y(t)_+^2.

Here x(t)>=T and E-t>0. The squared positive part is continuously
differentiable, including y=0. Direct differentiation gives

    d[H(t)^2/(E-t)^2]/dt
      =2[H(t)^2-(E-t)(u x(t)+(u^2/v)y(t)_+)]/(E-t)^3. (20)

When y(t)>=0, the parenthesis after E-t equals
u[T+A-t u(1+u^2/v^2)]<=u(T+A). When y(t)<0 it equals u x(t), also at
most u(T+A). Since u<=A/(M+zeta)<=A/M, (5) gives

    (E-t)(u x(t)+(u^2/v)y(t)_+)
         <=E u(T+A)<=A E(T+A)/M<=T^2<=H(t)^2.

The derivative in (20) is nonnegative. At t=0, H(0)=R_hom. Equations
(19)--(20) consequently prove the exact comparison needed:

    R102/B >= H(zeta)/(E-zeta) >= R_hom/E.          (21)

Under (6) the terminal numerator is multiplied by (E/B)^2. It is
positive, as proved after (12). Combining that identity with (21)
proves V102<=V_hom. This argument retains the nearest-minimizer reduction
and both branches of the positive part. It does not substitute the
uncentered initial norm for R102 and does not assume kappa=0.

## 7. Complete homogeneous-parameter bound used in the comparison

This section rederives the bound, rather than adopting unverified C101
as a premise. Let t=A-Mu in the legal homogeneous comparison. Then
0<=t<=A and (16) gives

    M^2v^2 <= t(M+A-t).

Completing the square in its final gradient magnitude gives

    V_hom <= (E^2/sigma) v^2/[(T+t)^2+M^2v^2].     (22)

For fixed t the fraction increases with v^2. Put C=M+A. The fraction
is thus at most

    Psi(t)=t(C-t)/[M^2(T^2+(2T+C)t)].              (23)

Its derivative has the sign of C T^2-2T^2t-(2T+C)t^2, whose unique
positive zero is t_0=T C/(2T+C). Therefore the maximum on[0,A] is
at min(A,t_0).

If A>=t_0, this maximum is C^2/[M^2(2T+C)^2], at most A^2/(M^2T^2)
by precisely A>=t_0. If A<=t_0, it is

    A/[M((T+A)^2+MA)] <= 1/(T+A+M)^2.              (24)

For the last inequality, with X=T+A the positive-denominator difference
is

    M(X^2+MA)-A(X+M)^2
       =X[M(T-A)-A(T+A)]>=0.

Its final sign is equivalent to A<=T(M+A)/(2T+M+A), exactly the branch
condition. Thus (22)--(24) prove (7) with the ORIGINAL prefix, middle and
suffix masses. No relaxed maximum is asserted attainable.

## 8. Every original H_m pair satisfies the new word condition

Use the unchanged enclosing-m parameters. Abbreviate a=a_m, c=c_m,
rho_c=c-2. The exact mass recurrence and its solution are

    M_0=1, M_(k+1)=2M_k+a c^k,
    M_k=[a/(c-2)]c^k+[1-a/(c-2)]2^k.             (25)

For m>=128,

    12/5<lambda<c<49/20,
    0<a<lambda-2<rho_c<=9/20,
    M_k<=c^k, M_(k+1)<=c M_k,
    a c^k<=rho_c M_k.                            (26)

Indeed sqrt(2)<10/7 gives
c<(17/7)(129/128)=2193/896<49/20. Also
a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda]<1/lambda=lambda-2<c-2.
Formula (25) then gives the remaining inequalities directly.

A level-k checkpoint has excess a_k=a c^k and has an immediately
preceding and following local copy of H_k. Its full prefix and suffix
masses plus1 are therefore at least M_k. For ANY pair i<j, of levels
k,l, let P,S be its original prefix and intervening masses. We claim

    Pair_ij=a_k a_l/[(1+P)(1+S)]
        <=q_pair:=c rho_c^2<1/2.                 (27)

If i precedes the entire copy H_l immediately before j, then
1+P>=M_k and1+S>=M_l, giving the stronger bound rho_c^2. In particular
this case includes k>=l, since a level at least l cannot lie in H_l.

Otherwise i lies inside that copy H_l and k<l. Let P_loc be its prefix
mass within this copy, x=1+P_loc and y=1+S. The adjacent H_k copies imply
x,y>=M_k, while x+y=M_l-a_k. Hence

    (1+P)(1+S)>=xy>=M_k(M_l-a_k-M_k).

Writing theta=a_k/M_k<=rho_c and z=M_l/M_k>=2+theta, one obtains

    Pair_ij <=theta rho_c z/(z-1-theta)
             <=theta rho_c(2+theta)
             <=rho_c^2(2+rho_c)=q_pair.

The middle inequality follows because z/(z-1-theta) decreases for
z>=2+theta. Finally q_pair<=(49/20)(9/20)^2=3969/8000<1/2.

For the same original pair, A/T<=rho_c by its local first-checkpoint
prefix bound. Thus

    A E(T+A)/(M T^2)
       =Pair_ij(1+A/T)
       <=q_pair(1+rho_c)<29/40<1.                 (28)

This proves (5) uniformly for every checkpoint partition, with no
assumed existence of a specially favorable pair.

## 9. Complete accepted same-word comparisons

First compare EVERY single cut. For a fixed level-k step h=1+a_k and
total original mass S_total=M_m-1, its denominator as a function of
its prefix mass P is

    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_total-h-P)].

Its derivative has the sign of2(S_total-h)-3P, so its minimum over the
interval from first to last occurrence is at an endpoint. The first
occurrence has prefix M_k-1 and suffix at least M_(m-1)-1. The last
has the reversed masses. Using a_k<=rho_c M_k and monotonicity of
x^2/(2x-1) for x>=1 in the last-occurrence case, every cut is at most
rho_c^2/(2M_(m-1)-1). It follows that

    Cut_i/[1/(2M_m-1)]
       <=rho_c^2(2c-1)<=3159/4000<1.              (29)

Here (2cX-1)/(2X-1)<=2c-1 for X>=1 and (26) were used.
Thus the COMPLETE accepted functional K_n(H_m) equals1/(2M_m-1).

Now compare the COMPLETE alternating-ray family from accepted
1fca3e5dd555cb4a. If a selection had at least two checkpoints i<j, its
first two nonterminal magnitudes would be m_0=1/(1+P) and
m_1=A m_0/(1+S). Its first old offset is P m_0. The budget after the
second checkpoint is

    E m_1-Pm_0=m_0[Pair_ij(1+P)-P]<m_0,            (30)

by (27). With exactly two checkpoints, feasibility requires at least
m_0 before the final ordinary block. With more checkpoints, the next
nonterminal magnitude is at most this budget and is less than m_0,
contradicting the accepted same-ray nondecreasing-magnitude condition.
Hence NO selection with two or more checkpoints is feasible. The zero-
and single-checkpoint families are exactly the K terms, proving

    F_ray_n(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=1/(2M_m-1).              (31)

To compare the pair term in (7) while retaining the original tail, put
p_0=1+P, x_0=1+S, t_0=1+U, and R_pair=Pair_ij. These are local mass
scalars, not trajectory points. Palindromicity and (27) applied in the
reverse order give A E/(t_0 x_0)<=q_pair. The two local sides also give
A<=rho_c p_0 and E<=rho_c t_0. Thus

    R_pair<=q_pair,
    p_0<=q_pair t_0/R_pair,
    x_0<=rho_c^2 t_0/R_pair.

The EXACT mass identity is M_m=p_0+x_0+t_0+A+E. Therefore

    R_pair <= min{q_pair,D_pair t_0/M_m},
    D_pair=2(1+rho_c)q_pair+rho_c^2.               (32)

For M_m>=8 the crossover t_*=q_pair M_m/D_pair exceeds1, because

    q_pair D_pair<=661/800,
    D_pair/q_pair=2(1+rho_c)+1/c<=199/60<4.

The first bound follows from q_pair<1/2 and
D_pair<29/20+81/400=661/400. The functions t^2/(2t-1) and1/(2t-1)
are increasing and decreasing respectively for t>=1. On the two sides
of t_*, (32) consequently yields

    R_pair^2/(2t_0-1)
       <=q_pair D_pair/(2M_m-D_pair/q_pair),
    [R_pair^2/(2t_0-1)]/[1/(2M_m-1)]
       <=(661/800)(2M_m-1)/(2M_m-199/60)
       <=5949/6088.                              (33)

The final rational function decreases with M_m and has the displayed
value at8. Combining (7), (28)--(33) proves (8) for ALL members of the
declared affine family and ALL pairs, including whole-word selections.

The accepted V_lens functional in dfde2fa326c908bf includes the same
Huber term. Therefore (8) also implies

    V102<=gamma102 V_lens(H_m).

This is a comparison to its COMPLETE applicable definition; no finite
selection of complex or quadratic parameters is substituted. It does
not assert that the complex part equals its Huber term. Likewise any
complete scalar/complex reference augmented by this same Huber witness
dominates the family. No equality or impossibility claim for all scalar
nonlinear functions or for unrestricted W is implied.

The accepted C59 fact e9a55f57461f253c concerns the ACTUAL enclosing-H6
construction. Our m>=128 theorem does not transfer C59 to subwords with
different enclosing parameters and makes no finite-H6 record claim.

## 10. Original all-split benchmark normalization

For clarity, the benchmark estimate is rederived from accepted
b98bb807d514e80d, with no replacement recurrence. Let rho=log_2(lambda)>1
and C_n=1+S_circle(n). The original mass joins are

    Omega(a_0,a_1)=(a_0+a_1+sqrt((a_0+a_1)^2+4a_0a_1))/2,
    G(a_0,d_0)=2a_0+d_0/2+sqrt(d_0^2+8a_0d_0)/2.

Put f_sc(t)=(1+t)^rho-1, t>0. As a function of log t its logarithm has
second derivative

    rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)
       [(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/f_sc(t)^2>0.

The bracket is positive by differentiating it from t=0. Midpoint
convexity at log t and -log t gives
f_sc(t)f_sc(1/t)>=f_sc(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2.
Consequently

    Omega(a_0,a_1)<=(a_0^(1/rho)+a_1^(1/rho))^rho.

For positive arguments this follows by substituting the right side X
in (X-a_0)(X-a_1)>=2a_0a_1, the positive-root equation for Omega.
Zero arguments follow by continuity. Induction in the ORIGINAL maximum
over ALL splits gives C_n<=(n+1)^rho. Since G(a_0,d_0)<=4a_0+d_0,
a second induction gives

    B_n<=1+4n^rho.                               (34)

For a split k+l+1=n, use (k+1)^rho+l^rho<=n^rho, obtained by integrating
the increasing derivative of x^rho. This proves the induction also when
l=0. No selected maximizing split or fitted dyadic sequence was used.

With the ACTUAL enclosing-m parameters, (25) gives

    M_m/lambda^m
      =(1+1/m)^m[a_m/(c_m-2)
          +(1-a_m/(c_m-2))(2/c_m)^m] -> exp(1).

Here a_m/(c_m-2)->1 and2/c_m is uniformly below1. Therefore

    B_n sup V102
      <=gamma102[1+4(2^m-1)^rho]/(2M_m-1)
       ->2gamma102/exp(1)<1,                     (35)

which proves (9). The COMPLETE accepted scalar/ray reference on this
same word itself has limsup B_n K_n(H_m)<=2/exp(1)<1. Thus these words
are precisely an applicable region where the existing scalar/ray
channel misses the original target, and the new affine family remains
strictly weaker. This says nothing about the actual unrestricted W.

## 11. Exact positive-reserve example and continuation audit

Use the original word h=(1,5,1,5,1), with the two5's as checkpoints, and

    S_0=S_1=S_2=1, zeta=1, u=1/4, v=1,
    ell=1/2, kappa=1/2.

Then D=3, B=3, delta=13/4, Z=2. All of (2) hold, with positive endpoint
reserves13/4,9/16,1. The whole physical trajectory is

    index  point                  gradient  value
    0      (21/4,-3)              g_A       19/4
    1      (17/4,-3)              g_A       15/4
    2      (-3/4,-3)              g_B       69/32
    3      (-1/2,-2)              g_B       35/32
    4      (3/4,3)                g_C       11/8
    5      (3/4,5/2)              g_C       9/8.

The closest minimizer is(0,-1/2); hence

    R102^2=541/16, V102=36/541.

At kappa=0 the same point/gradient sequence is legal but the minimum is
uniquely0, the two middle values are larger by1/2, and the true squared
radius is585/16. Its payoff is36/585=4/65. Thus the intercept has a REAL
nearest-minimizer normalization benefit at fixed trajectory. It cannot
be dismissed by keeping the old radius. This finite word does not
satisfy (5), so it is not offered as an instance of that domination
condition.

The complete accepted K_5 is max{1/27,4/15,1/12}=4/15. For the accepted
ray family, a size1 checkpoint cannot precede another checkpoint because
the next required magnitude is nonpositive. A final size1 checkpoint
cannot produce a positive two-return budget; when it is the only
checkpoint it contributes zero. Thus only the two5's can appear in an
improving selection. Selecting both gives
m_0=1/2,m_1=1,O_1=1/2,z_term=7/2,e_term=1/4,q_opt=7/6,
with payoff55/12. Together with all zero/single choices, this proves
F_ray_5=55/12. The example is weaker than BOTH complete references.

The exact carry in (14) is1, whereas the zero-plane reserve alone would
suggest4. Appending a single physical step2 sends the old terminal point
to(3/4,3/2). A proposed unchanged g_C would have proximal point(3/4,1),
where the old g_A-plane is3/4 and g_C-plane1/2. Equation (12) gives the
violated remote row Q_(0,6)=1/4>0, despite remaining positive zero-plane
reserve. For the SAME static function, its actual gradient is instead
(1/5,2/5). Indeed its proximal point is(11/20,11/10); the g_A- and
g_C-planes tie at11/20, the zero and g_B-planes are lower, and
(1/5,2/5)=(1/5)g_A+(4/5)g_C. Formula (10) proves this exact gradient,
and its function value is11/20+1/10=13/20.

For every allowed holding append0<=t_extra<=1, (15) is exactly
V102(t_extra)=(36-8t_extra)/541. An illegal requested continuation is
not described as an actual trajectory.

## 12. The word condition is not silently removable

Here is an exact counterexample to the claim that conversion (6) always
dominates, with no word condition. It is not a counterexample to (5)--(9)
or a gain over the complete accepted references.

Take h=(5,5), so T=M=N=sigma=1 and A=E=4. Set

    u=1, v=sqrt(3/2), zeta=1/2, kappa=1,
    D=3/2, B=7/2, delta=5/2, ell=7v/4.

The first inequality is Dv^2=9/4<=6=(1+u)delta+kappa.
The middle reserve is zeta Q-kappa=1/4>0. The final reserve is
ell Z=147/32>=7/2=Bu. Thus the whole law is legal, with all three
endpoint reserves positive and all directed rows proved by (12).
Its points are

    X_0=(7/2,-3v/2), X_1=(-3/2,-3v/2),
    X_2=(7/2,7v/2).

The closest minimizer is(0,-1/v), so

    R102^2=49/4+25/24=319/24,
    2F102(X_2)=441/32,
    V102=1323/1276.

The specific conversion (6) gives zeta'=kappa'=0, ell'=2v,
X'_0=(4,-v), true squared radius35/2, and terminal numerator18.
Its payoff is36/35. The strict inequality

    1323/1276 > 36/35

follows from1323*35-36*1276=369>0. Thus deleting (5) from the proposed
conversion theorem would be false. We do NOT claim that (5) is necessary
for domination on every pair, nor that this counterexample beats the
maximum over all homogeneous parameters.

The complete same-word accepted K_2 is16/11. All ray choices consist of
no checkpoint, one of the two5's, or both. The two-checkpoint choice has
m_0=1,m_1=4,z_term=16,m_old=1,q_opt=16 and payoff256. Therefore the
complete F_ray_2 is256, and the scalar quadratic f(x)=x^2/2 also gives
256. This example is an assumption-scope diagnostic only.

## 13. A precise nonradial interface, with the profile comparison still open

To specify the geometric gap rather than merely naming it, keep the same
three gradient directions and the SAME definition of J102,F102, but
replace the common radial middle reserve by independent alpha,beta>=0.
These two scalars are local proximal coordinates, not enclosing-word
parameters. Put

    delta_nr=A-Mu-alpha>=0,
    X_0_nr=(T+delta_nr)e_1-(Mv+beta)e_2,
    C_nr=Eu-alpha, Z_nr=Ev-beta-Nell.

All parameters u>=0,v>0,ell>0,kappa>=0 are as before. The entire
three-block gradient law is legal EXACTLY when

    v(Mv+beta)<=(1+u)delta_nr+kappa,
    kappa<=u alpha+v beta,
    ell Z_nr>=max{0,C_nr,-u C_nr-v Z_nr-kappa}.       (36)

Here is the complete activity check for this new interface. The last
first-block proximal point is(delta_nr,-Mv-beta), where the four planes
compare by the first condition, delta_nr>=0, and negativity of the
g_C-plane. Earlier points add a nonnegative multiple of g_A. The first
checkpoint sends the actual point to(-Mu-alpha,-Mv-beta). At cumulative
middle mass t, its proximal point is

    -[alpha+(S_1-t)u]e_1-[beta+(S_1-t)v]e_2.

At the middle endpoint the g_B-plane height is u alpha+v beta-kappa;
the other nonzero planes have heights -alpha and -ell beta. Thus the
second condition makes g_B active there and at every earlier middle
query. The second checkpoint sends the point to(C_nr,Ev-beta).
The terminal proximal point is(C_nr,Z_nr), and the third condition is
exactly all its g_C-plane comparisons. Earlier tail points add a
nonnegative multiple of e_2, improving every comparison. Necessity uses
the exposed-slope argument from Section3. Hence (12) proves EVERY
directed row of this enlarged interface as well.

The actual minimizer set remains K102, so its nearest point and exact
payoff are

    x_star_nr=(0,-min{Mv+beta,kappa/v}),
    R_nr^2=(T+delta_nr)^2+(Mv+beta-kappa/v)_+^2,
    V_nr=[2ell(Ev-beta)-sigma ell^2]/R_nr^2.         (37)

The same coordinatewise nearest-point proof and active-plane value
formula prove these identities. For a same-function holding append the
three terminal comparisons in (36) must all survive. Its EXACT total
mass allowance is the nonnegative quantity

    Tcarry_nr=min{Z_nr/ell,
                  (ell Z_nr-C_nr)/ell^2,
                  [(ell+v)Z_nr+u C_nr+kappa]/[ell(ell+v)]}.

Each entry is nonnegative by (36). Replace Z_nr by Z_nr-t ell to check
each of the three rows individually; earlier appended partial masses
are smaller. Its payoff decreases by exactly2t ell^2/R_nr^2.

The radial class is the subcase alpha=zeta u,beta=zeta v. Eliminating
kappa between the first two conditions of (36) gives the necessary
bound

    Mv^2<=(1+u)(A-Mu)-alpha.                       (38)

The same elimination in the true-radius formula gives

    R_nr^2 >=(T+A-Mu-alpha)^2+(Mv-u alpha/v)_+^2.   (39)

These are immediate algebraic consequences of the exact interface,
not a new all-h lower bound. In particular beta can now be zero while
alpha is positive. The right side of (39) then decreases with alpha
without the linked loss beta=zeta v in the terminal vertical budget
Ev-beta. Thus the radius-versus-terminal monotonicity in (20) does not
transfer by merely relabeling a radial parameter.

This is a concrete starting interface for a DIFFERENT family. Its full
same-word optimum on H_m, a strong whole-word parameter selection, and
a scalable sequence of changed-gradient checkpoints remain OPEN. We do
not assert that (36) has a benchmark-strength member, nor that (8) holds
for this larger nonradial class. Only its exact static realization,
physical rows, true-radius identities and holding price are derived
here. The new interface is finite planar geometry with three prescribed
gradients, not unrestricted Gram feasibility in alternative notation.

## 14. Scope, grounding, and remaining interface

The family pays a transverse noncore initial anchor, an independent
middle affine intercept, a nontrivial minimizer triangle/strip, all three
value reserves, every physical step, every directed interpolation row,
and exact same-function holding continuation. Every partition and every
parameter in its stated class is covered by (8). No fixed-past universal
recovery hypothesis is imposed, and none of the original enclosing-m
parameters, horizon, or B_n normalization changes.

Its geometric scope remains: two changes of gradient, first/final
gradient directions orthogonal, middle gradient in the opposite quadrant,
and a radial middle trajectory about the common zero of the first/final
planes. Within that scope the middle intercept kappa is unrestricted
except for exact legality. More general nonradial middle residuals,
other endpoint angles, and scalable many-checkpoint laws are NOT excluded.

In particular, a nonradial middle endpoint would introduce independent
coordinates in its proximal residual. The shared intercept can then pay
different parts of the initial transverse distance without the same
zeta cost in the terminal vertical budget. Equation (20) specifically
uses their common radial parameter; it is not a proof for that larger
class. Section13 supplies exact physical-row and radius formulas for
one such enlargement, but no strong whole-word selection or profile
comparison for it. It must not be folded into the obstruction (8).

The direct-proving screen supplied explicit proofs of the static-law,
true-radius, all-query, legal-selection, paid-continuation and entire-
stated-family-obstruction subgoals within this UNVERIFIED document. The
profile-strength positive construction failed for the whole stated
family by (35). Counterexample Section12 refutes only the unconditioned
conversion; Section11 refutes only reserve-against-zero continuation.

Literature grounding used Matlas in active-program/mutation mode. Its
Moreau-envelope result in2303.08785 and short-step self-contraction result
in2003.04201 do not supply this long-step law. The local exact paper
downloads/taylor-hendrickx-glineur-1502.05666.txt was read at Section2.5,
Theorem4, its proof and construction, and Corollary1. The setting there
specializes to finite Euclidean data, convexity, and Lipschitz gradient1.
The accepted mathematical input is1252b8823bb5a290, and the needed affine
Moreau construction is independently derived in Sections2--3. No new
external black box or unverified shared lemma is used as a brick.

No numerical evaluation, solver, machine algebra, replay, formal verifier,
installation, consult, or subagent was used. Operational text/hash checks
are persistence checks, not mathematical verification. This return
exhausts the two-round allocation at completed33; no older resource or
round renews itself. The original all-h/profile target remains OPEN.

# Near-critical ruler: complete quadratic screen and a quantitative cap obstruction

Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No solver, numerical sweep, or
fact_submit was used. All new estimates are proved here; prior unverified
findings are not premises. Accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d` supplies the
definitions and validity of K_n and the exact benchmark recursions; accepted
fact `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies smooth-convex interpolation. This does not
upper-bound the unrestricted W_n and does not settle the project target.

## 1. Exact schedule and all quadratic curvatures, including arbitrary b

Fix an integer m>=2. Within this document write

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),
    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.

The parameters c,a remain fixed for this entire horizon. They are not
recomputed when j changes. The symbol H_j here names a schedule, not the
conjugate jet quadratic from the project glossary. Put r=c-2 and A_mass=a/r.
Counting the occurrences of each level gives, for 0<=j<=m,

    S_j=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2),
    M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j.                       (1)

There are 2^(m-j-1) occurrences of d_j in H_m. Its scalar multiplier at
curvature t in [0,1] is exactly

    P_m(t)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j t)^(2^(m-j-1)).     (2)

Let p_m be H_m without its final d_0 and let h^(m,b)=(p_m,b), b>=0. Then

    sum h^(m,b)=S_m-d_0+b,
    R_(m,b)(t)=(1-bt) product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j t)^N_j,
    N_0=2^(m-1)-1, N_j=2^(m-j-1) for 1<=j<m.           (3)

The exact worst normalized quadratic objective over ALL finite dimensions
is

    Q_m(b)=max_(0<=t<=1) t R_(m,b)(t)^2.                (4)

Indeed diagonalizing a positive semidefinite Hessian bounded by the identity
makes the normalized error a weighted average of the displayed scalar
quantities. A scalar quadratic attains the maximum. Zero eigenvalues have
zero objective contribution; affine translations to a minimizer change
neither calculation. Thus this is a full quadratic-class computation.

For an exact finite characterization of (4), subdivide [0,1] at all its
distinct interior roots 1/d_j and, when b>0, 1/b. On each root-free open
interval the logarithmic derivative of the nonzero objective is

    L(t)=1/t-2b/(1-bt)-2sum_j N_j d_j/(1-d_j t),
    L'(t)=-1/t^2-2b^2/(1-bt)^2
                    -2sum_j N_j d_j^2/(1-d_j t)^2<0.   (5)

Consequently there is exactly one interior maximizer between consecutive
roots, also between zero and the first root. On the last interval the
maximum is either its unique root of L or t=1. Values at roots are zero.
Coincident roots are combined, and b=0 simply removes its term. Equations
(3)--(5) specify the complete spectrum without a numerical sweep.

The unit-curvature values alone are

    P_m(1)=-(ac)^(2^m-1)c^(-m),
    R_(m,b)(1)=(1-b)(ac)^(2^m-2)c^(1-m).                (6)

They are not used as a replacement for (4).

## 2. Exact single-cut screen for every appended b

For a cut with step d, preceding mass P and following mass T, the accepted
cut value is (d-1)_+^2/[(1+P)^2(1+2T)]. For a fixed d and total schedule
mass S, its reciprocal denominator is

    D(P)=(1+P)^2[1+2(S-d-P)].

Its derivative has the sign of 2(S-d)-3P. Therefore its minimum on any
closed interval is attained at an endpoint. Among all occurrences of a
given level it suffices to check the first and last occurrence.

For h^(m,b), all levels 1<=j<m remain present. Their first and last
preceding masses are, respectively,

    P_j^first=S_j, P_j^last=S_m-d_j-S_j.                (7)

Their following masses are S_m-d_0+b-d_j-P. At level zero, after deleting
the original last occurrence, the endpoint preceding masses are

    P_0^first=0, P_0^last=S_m-2d_0-d_1,                 (8)

with following masses S_m-2d_0+b and d_1+b. For m=2 the two occurrences
in (8) refer to the same remaining index. The appended b contributes

    (b-1)_+^2/(1+S_m-d_0)^2.                           (9)

Taking the maximum of the cuts in (7)--(9) and
1/[1+2(S_m-d_0+b)] is EXACTLY K_n(h^(m,b)). No omitted internal cut can
be larger, by the denominator argument. In particular the accepted final
cut already proves W_n(h^(m,b))>=1/B_n whenever

    b>=1+(1+S_m-d_0)/sqrt(B_n).                        (10)

The global Huber term proves the same comparison whenever
b<=(B_n-1)/2-(S_m-d_0), if that right side is nonnegative. These tests use
the actual B_n, not a guessed dyadic constant.

## 3. An exact all-cut result at the original final step

For the remainder take m>=128 unless indicated, and first set b=d_0, so
the actual schedule is H_m. The following rational bounds will be used:

    12/5<lambda<c<49/20<5/2,
    3/8<a<lambda-2<1/2,
    ac>=127/128>7/8,
    0<a<c-2=r<=9/20.                                   (11)

For example lambda<17/7 gives
c<2193/896<49/20 for m>=128; a=(1-1/m)/c then exceeds3/8.
The upper bound a<lambda-2 follows from a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda].
Consequently 0<A_mass<1, and (1) gives

    M_j<=c^j, M_(j+1)<=c M_j,
    a c^j<=r M_j.                                      (12)

Let M=M_(m-1). For a first occurrence of any level j, P=S_j and its
following mass is at least S_(m-1). Its cut is at most
r^2/(2M-1). For a last occurrence, its preceding mass is at least
S_(m-1), and its following mass is S_j. Since x^2/(2x-1) is increasing
for x>=1 and M_j<=M, its cut is also at most

    r^2 M_j^2/[M^2(2M_j-1)]<=r^2/(2M-1).              (13)

These statements include the unique central occurrence, for which the
first and last positions coincide. Using c<=5/2, r<=1/2 and M>=1,

    r^2(2M_m-1)<= (5M-1)/4 <= 2M-1.

Thus every single-cut term is no larger than the global Huber term:

    K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).                               (14)

## 4. Full-spectrum quadratic bound at b=d_0

This argument controls every t in [0,1], not only t=1. Write
v_j=a c^j t and, for the partial ruler H_j,

    Y_j=c v_j |P_j(t)|.

If no level 0,...,m-1 satisfies d_j t>=1, all factors in (2) are
nonnegative, and 1-u<=exp(-u) gives

    t P_m(t)^2<=t exp(-2S_m t)<=1/(2e S_m).             (15)

The last maximum is over all nonnegative t, so it also bounds the actual
restricted interval. Suppose instead that j is the first level with
d_j t>=1. If j=0, Y_0=ac t<=1. If j>=1, all factors in P_j are
nonnegative and t<=1/(1+a)<=8/11. Equations (1), (11) give

    t S_j>=v_j/r-t,
    Y_j<=c v_j exp(-v_j/r+t)<=cr exp(t-1)<1.            (16)

Here the maximum of v exp(-v/r) is r/e, cr<5/4, and
exp(3/11)>1+3/11=14/11>5/4. For every subsequent level l>=j the
factor is on its nonpositive side, and the exact recursion is

    Y_(l+1)=[1-(1-t)/v_l]Y_l^2<=Y_l^2<=1.             (17)

The bracket lies in [0,1]. If an earlier factor is zero the conclusion
holds directly as well. Therefore

    t P_m(t)^2<=1/[c^2 a^2 c^(2m)t]
                      <=d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m)),      (18)

using t>=1/d_(m-1). Equations (15),(18) prove the explicit full-spectrum
estimate

    Q_m(d_0)<=max{1/(2e S_m),
                 (1+a c^(m-1))/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))}.      (19)

Both entries are strictly below (14). For the first, S_m>=1 gives
(2S_m+1)/(2e S_m)<=3/(2e)<3/4. For the second, using (12) and ac>7/8,

    (2S_m+1)(1+a c^(m-1))/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))
       <=2/[(ac)^2 c^m]+2/[c^2 ac]
       <=16/49+25/63=2233/3087<3/4.                   (20)

For the first term of this last bound it already suffices that m>=3
and c>2. Thus, with considerable slack,

    Q_m(d_0)<(3/4)K_n(H_m).                            (21)

## 5. Exact benchmark comparison, with no fitted constant

Let rho=log_2(lambda). The accepted benchmark defines the primitive mass
C_n=S_circle(n)+1 with join

    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,

and B_n with join G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. We prove directly
from these recursions that

    C_n<=(n+1)^rho, B_n<=1+4n^rho.                     (22)

Put F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1, t>0. Direct differentiation, with t=exp(z), gives

    d^2/dz^2 log F(exp z)
      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.

The strict inequality follows by integrating the positive derivative of
(1+t)^rho-1-rho t for t>0. Convexity at z and -z gives
F(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. If A=t^rho B and
D=(1+t)^rho B, this says (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, or
D^2-(A+B)D-AB>=0. Hence

    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.

Zero arguments follow by continuity. Induction in the primitive
recursion proves the first bound in (22). Since G(A,D)<=4A+D,
induction in the dominant recursion, with a split k and l=n-k-1,
gives B_n<=1+4[(k+1)^rho+l^rho]<=1+4n^rho. The last inequality follows
by integrating the increasing derivative of x^rho, including l=0.

In our family (1) implies

    M_m/lambda^m
       =(1+1/m)^m[A_mass+(1-A_mass)(2/c)^m] -> e,        (23)

since A_mass->1 and 2/c is bounded below1. Thus the EXACT finite bound

    K_n(H_m) B_n
      <=[1+4(2^m-1)^rho]/(2M_m-1)                     (24)

has limit 2/e<1. This uses an upper bound derived for the actual B_n
at every integer horizon; it assumes no asymptotic phase or decimal
value for B_n/lambda^m. In particular, the full quadratic and all-cut
baseline is strictly below U_n^ZJ for every sufficiently large m.

## 6. Retained two-block geometry and all constant tails in its cap range

Here is the precise mechanism tested next. For ANY two selected cuts i<j
of an arbitrary schedule, let P be the mass before i, S the mass strictly
between i and j, T the mass after j, and define

    M_0=1+P, M_1=1+S,
    A=(h_i-1)/M_0, epsilon=(h_j-1)/M_1, sigma=2T+1.

Assume h_i,h_j>1 and choose the cap-range angle

    0<kappa<=A/(1+epsilon).                            (25)

Use the retained prefix gradients and proximal point

    p=(1,0)/M_0,
    q=(-kappa,sqrt(kappa(A-kappa)))/M_1,
    X=(-A,0), y=X-M_1 q, d=-M_1 q.                    (26)

These prefix data are actual jets of the single Moreau envelope of
J(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>), starting at (1,0). Before the first checkpoint
the proximal points are (1-(u+1)/M_0,0), 0<=u<=P, so the p-plane is
active. That checkpoint, of size1+A M_0, sends its input p to X. In
the second block, the proposed proximal points X-tq, 1<=t<=M_1, have

    <q,X-tq>=(A kappa/M_1)(1-t/M_1)>=0,
    <q-p,X-tq>
      =A kappa/M_1+A/M_0
          -t[A kappa/M_1^2+kappa/(M_0 M_1)]
      >=(A-kappa)/M_0>0.

This verifies every actual old query, even if individual ordinary steps
are large. At the second checkpoint its jet is

    x_j=y+q, g_j=q, f_j=||q||^2/2.

For completeness, the envelope minimizes J(z)+||x-z||^2/2. This has a
unique minimizer, and an active-plane slope g at z proves optimality at
x=z+g. At two proximal points, monotonicity of the subgradients gives
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, hence a 1-Lipschitz gradient. The two minimizing
inequalities identify the derivative as g; joint convexity proves
convexity of the envelope. Thus the claimed function has the required
global regularity, not only valid formal data.

The second checkpoint, of size1+M_1 epsilon, sends the iterate to
X_tail=y+epsilon d. The optimum jet is (0,0,0). Every interpolant of
this prefix has origin as an actual closest minimizer to (1,0): the
retained first checkpoint jet (p,p,||p||^2/2), interpolated with any
minimizer z, forces <p,z><=0. Its squared distance from (1,0) is
therefore at least1, attained at origin. This normalization persists
for all extensions below.

Allow ANY legal continuation with a single common gradient g at every
tail query, including the terminal sample; allow affine intercepts,
positive values and extra dimensions, and retain every prefix jet.
Its endpoint is y+epsilon d-Tg. The accepted interpolation inequality
from this endpoint to the old jet (y+q,q,||q||^2/2) gives

    2f_end<=2epsilon<d,g>-sigma||g||^2
                         <=epsilon^2||d||^2/sigma
                         =A kappa epsilon^2/sigma.

This uses a retained old query, not a reset to a new problem. From (25),
EVERY such constant-tail continuation obeys the exact universal cap

    2f_end<=U_ij:=A^2 epsilon^2/[(1+epsilon)(1+2T)].     (27)

In particular it covers all angles and all optimized tail planes of the
previous explicit two-checkpoint cap policy. It does not cover angles
kappa>A/(1+epsilon), different two-block prefixes, or variable-gradient
tails. No claim that (27) is attained for every pair is needed below.

## 7. The complete cap-range mechanism is dominated by K on H_m

Return to the full H_m, m>=128. For a selected pair i<j let their levels
be u,v; write d=a c^v=h_j-1, x=1+S, and epsilon=d/x. The individual
accepted cut terms at i and j are K_i,K_j. Direct cancellation gives

    U_ij/K_i=epsilon^2/(1+epsilon)
                          +2d epsilon/(1+2T),          (28)
    U_ij/K_j=(h_i-1)^2(1+P+h_i+S)^2
                          /[(1+P)^2 x(x+d)].           (29)

Every occurrence of level v has a full H_v immediately after it, so
T>=S_v. If v>=2 then M_v>=M_2=4+2a+ac>5, and by (11),(12),

    2d/(1+2T)<=2r M_v/(2M_v-1)<=10r/9<=1/2.

Consequently, for epsilon<=1, (28) is at most1.

If epsilon>1, then x<d. The first selected cut must lie INSIDE the
copy of H_v immediately preceding j and its level satisfies u<v.
Otherwise that copy lies between the cuts, giving S>=S_v and
x>=M_v>d. Inside this copy put P_loc for its mass before i. The
subcopies of H_u immediately before and after i yield

    P>=P_loc, x>=M_u,
    1+P_loc=M_v-(h_i-1)-x.

Also h_i-1<=d/c<=r M_v/c and x<d<=r M_v. For fixed x, the factor
(1+P+h_i+S)/(1+P) decreases with P, so substituting P_loc in (29)
gives

    U_ij/K_j
      <=(h_i-1)^2 M_v^2/
          [(M_v-(h_i-1)-x)^2 x(x+d)]
      <r^2/[2(1-r-r/c)^2]
      <=648/841<1.                                    (30)

The penultimate step uses h_i-1<=r M_u<=r x and x/(x+d)<1/2.
For the last, r<=9/20 and c>=12/5 imply 1-r-r/c>=29/80.

It remains to include the two small levels. If v=0, then d=a<1/2 and
epsilon<=d. Equation (28) is at most d^2/(1+d)+2d^2<=2/3. If v=1,
then d=ac<1. Unless i is the one level-zero step immediately before j,
the intervening mass includes H_1, so epsilon<=1/2 and T>=S_1>=1;
(28) is then at most1/6+1/3=1/2. For that one adjacent pair x=1 and
h_i-1=a. Equation (29) is bounded by

    a^2(2+a)^2/(1+ac)<=1/(1+ac)<1,

because a<=sqrt(2)-1 implies a(2+a)<=1. This covers EVERY ordered pair,
including pairs near the end and repeated occurrences of either level.

Combining (14),(27)--(30), every member of the complete constant-tail
mechanism in Section6 has normalized error at most K_n(H_m). Therefore

    max{Q_m(d_0), K_n(H_m),
        supremum of the Section6 constant-tail scores}
      =1/(2S_m+1),
    limsup B_n times this maximum <=2/e<1.             (31)

This is a quantitative failure of a concrete retained-history mechanism
on the ASSIGNED constant-level family. Its tail bound allows all
dimensions, intercepts and positive values once its prefix and angle
range are fixed. Thus retuning only its two cuts, its angle inside (25),
or its final constant-gradient plane cannot produce the target comparison
for all appended b: b=d_0 already defeats that possibility.

The unrestricted W_n may be larger. In particular, (31) gives no upper
certificate for it, no primitive-validity conclusion, and no decision of
the original all-schedule/all-horizon problem. The remaining exact branch
is to leave (25), change more than two prefix cells, or allow genuinely
varying gradients within the remaining ruler block while retaining all
queried jets and the closest-minimizer normalization.

## 8. The entire accepted alternating-ray family also collapses to K

We now invoke the exact ray recurrence from accepted fact
`1fca3e5dd555cb4a`. No unverified ray exclusion is used. For any two
indices i<j in H_m, write P,S for their preceding and intervening masses,
and let u,v be their levels. Put r_u=a c^u and r_v=a c^v. We claim

    r_u r_v<(1+P)(1+S).                               (32)

If u>=v, or if i lies before the full H_v immediately preceding j,
the local copies give 1+P>=M_u and 1+S>=M_v. Thus (32) follows from
r_u<=r M_u, r_v<=r M_v and r<1.

Otherwise u<v and i lies inside that preceding H_v. Define P_loc there.
The local H_u copies on both sides of i give

    1+P_loc>=M_u, 1+S>=M_u,
    (1+P_loc)+(1+S)=M_v-r_u.

Expanding [(1+P_loc)-M_u][(1+S)-M_u]>=0 shows

    (1+P)(1+S)>=M_u(M_v-r_u-M_u).

For fixed u define D_v=M_u(M_v-r_u-M_u)-r_u r_v.
At v=u+1, the identity M_(u+1)=2M_u+r_u gives

    D_(u+1)=M_u^2-c r_u^2
               >=(1-cr^2)M_u^2>0,

since cr^2<=3969/8000<1. At successive levels,

    D_(v+1)-D_v=M_u M_v+[M_u-(c-1)r_u]r_v>0,

because (c-1)r<=261/400<1. This proves (32) for every pair, including
the small levels with empty local blocks.

Suppose a ray construction selected at least two checkpoints i,j. Its
first two gradients and first old offset are

    m_0=1/(1+P), m_1=r_u m_0/(1+S), O_1=P m_0.

By (32), the second-return budget satisfies

    r_v m_1-O_1
       =m_0[r_u r_v/(1+S)-P]<m_0.                    (33)

With exactly two checkpoints, the terminal old-slope constraint requires
this quantity to be at least (1+T)m_0>=m_0. With three or more, the next
nonterminal gradient is this quantity divided by1 plus its block mass,
and hence is strictly below m_0, violating same-ray slope monotonicity.
Thus no accepted ray construction has two or more checkpoints. The
zero- and one-checkpoint cases recover K exactly, so

    F_ray_n(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).                  (34)

## 9. The complete universal complex lens is also below Huber

This concerns the accepted Lens_n and maximal path-value Gap_h from fact
`dfde2fa326c908bf`, not the larger schedule-dependent domain D_h. The
accepted bound is

    Gap_(H_m)(z)<=(2alpha-|z|^2)|P_m(z)|^2,
    z=alpha+i beta,
    kappa_lens=alpha-|z|^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2)).        (35)

All chronological prefix jets and their interpolation cycles remain those
of the accepted definition. We only bound their actual terminal polynomial.
We prove, for m>=128,

    sup_(z in Lens_n) Gap_(H_m)(z)<1/(2S_m+1).         (36)

The endpoints z=0 and z=1 follow directly: the former has zero error,
and at the latter the upper bound in (35) is P_m(1)^2, covered by (21).
Every other lens point has 0<alpha<1 and kappa_lens>0. Put

    delta=tan(pi/(n+2))<=1/100,
    tau_z=|1-z|^2/(2kappa_lens),
    q_decay=99/100.

The delta bound follows from tan x<=2x for 0<=x<=1/2 and pi<4,
since n=2^m-1. The tangent inequality follows from sin x<=x and
cos x>=1-x^2/2>=7/8 on that interval. From the lens inequality,

    |beta|<=delta alpha(1-alpha),
    kappa_lens>=alpha(1-alpha)(1-delta^2/4),
    tau_z<=F_delta(1-alpha)/(2alpha),
    F_delta=(1+delta^2)/(1-delta^2/4)<101/100.           (37)

Consider a partial ruler H_j all of whose levels l<j satisfy
a c^l<tau_z, with j>=1. In particular tau_z>a>3/8, and (37) gives
alpha<101/176<3/5. Each step d_l of H_j satisfies

    d_l alpha<(1+tau_z)alpha
       <=alpha+F_delta(1-alpha)/2<81/100.              (38)

Its real factor is therefore positive, at least19/100. The inequalities
log(1-u)<=-u and log(1+u)<=u imply

    log|1-d_l z|
      <=-d_l alpha+d_l^2 beta^2/[2(19/100)^2].

Sum with the exact multiplicities of the partial ruler. Its sum of
squared steps is at most its largest step times S_j, and (38) bounds
that largest step by81/(100alpha). Hence

    |P_j(z)|<=exp[-(1-12delta^2)alpha S_j]
                       <=exp(-q_decay alpha S_j).    (39)

For the numerical coefficient here,
(81/100)/[2(19/100)^2]=4050/361<12, and
1-12/10000>99/100. This is a symbolic bound; no spectral sampling occurs.

If no level l<m reaches a c^l>=tau_z, (39) applies to H_m. From (35),

    Gap_(H_m)(z)<=2alpha exp(-2q_decay S_m alpha)
                        <=1/(q_decay e S_m).

Since S_m>=2 and e>8/3, its ratio to Huber is strictly less than

    (5/2)/(q_decay(8/3))=125/132<1.                   (40)

Otherwise let j<m be the first level with a c^j>=tau_z. Put
Y_j=c a c^j |z| |P_j(z)|. If j=0, Y_0=ac|z|<=ac<1.
If j>=1, (39) and S_j>=a c^j/r-1 give, by maximizing
v exp(-q_decay v/r) over v>=0,

    Y_j<= [cr sqrt(1+delta^2)/q_decay]
                              exp(q_decay alpha-1)
       <=(9/8)exp(-2/5)<1.                           (41)

Indeed cr<=441/400, sqrt(1+delta^2)<=10001/10000,
and dividing their product by99/100 gives a number below9/8.
Also alpha<3/5 and exp(2/5)>1+2/5>9/8.

For every level l>=j, the definition of tau_z gives exactly

    |1-d_l z|^2
      =a^2 c^(2l)|z|^2+|1-z|^2-2a c^l kappa_lens
      <=a^2 c^(2l)|z|^2.

Consequently the ruler recursion implies Y_(l+1)<=Y_l^2, so Y_m<=1.
If alpha>1/16, (35) and |z|>=alpha imply that the ratio of its bound
to Huber is at most

    (2S_m+1) 32/[c^2 a^2 c^(2m)]
      <=64/[(ac)^2 c^m]<128/2^m<1.                   (42)

If alpha<=1/16, then tau_z>=(1-alpha)/(2alpha)>=15/2>a,
so j>=1. The same estimate before (41) now gives

    Y_j<=(9/8)exp(1/16)/e
                  <(9/8)(16/15)(3/8)=9/20<1/2.

Here exp(t)<=1/(1-t) for 0<=t<1 follows termwise from its series,
and e>8/3 follows by retaining its terms through degree four.
There is at least one update after index j, hence Y_m<1/4. The fact
that tau_z<=a c^(m-1), together with
|1-z|^2>=1-2alpha and kappa_lens<=alpha, yields

    alpha>=1/[2(1+a c^(m-1))]=1/(2d_(m-1)).

Equation (35) is therefore at most

    1/[8c^2 a^2 c^(2m)alpha]
       <=d_(m-1)/[4c^2 a^2 c^(2m)].                  (43)

By (20), its ratio to Huber is less than3/16. Equations (40)--(43)
cover every lens point and prove (36). Consequently

    V_lens(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=F_ray_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).      (44)

Combining (21), (31), (34), (44) gives the same exact 2/e limit-superior
obstruction even after including ALL accepted single-cut, alternating-ray,
and universal-lens baselines, as well as the complete quadratic class and
the retained two-block constant-tail mechanism from Section6. The full
schedule-dependent complex domain, general multidimensional nonlinear
orbits, and variable-gradient tails remain outside this statement.

## 10. Remove the angle restriction: every constant tail of this two-block prefix

The cap-angle condition (25) is not needed for the following stronger
obstruction. Allow EVERY 0<=kappa<=A in the two-block prefix (26), and
retain all its actual jets and the optimum jet. The prefix legality
calculation in Section6 still holds: the only strict comparison there
becomes nonnegative at kappa=A. At kappa=0 the second block is already
at a minimum. The argument proving origin is the closest minimizer is
unchanged. For ANY legal constant-gradient tail, (27)'s derivation before
using the cap range gives

    2f_end<=A kappa epsilon^2/(1+2T)
                  <=A^2 epsilon^2/(1+2T).             (45)

No claim that this outgoing upper bound is attainable is made. We show
its right side is itself smaller than the old Huber value on H_m.

First strengthen (32). Define q_pair=c r^2<1/2, r=c-2 as before.
For ANY selected i<j, with their levels u,v and preceding/intervening
masses P,S, one has

    R_pair:=(h_i-1)(h_j-1)/[(1+P)(1+S)]<=q_pair.       (46)

In the outside-copy case of Section8 the bound r^2 already suffices.
In the inside-copy case its denominator is at least
M_u(M_v-r_u-M_u). Write theta=r_u/M_u<=r and z=M_v/M_u.
Here z>=M_(u+1)/M_u=2+theta and r_v<=r M_v. Consequently

    R_pair<=theta r z/(z-1-theta)
              <=theta r(2+theta)<=r^2(2+r)=q_pair.    (47)

The middle inequality follows because z/(z-1-theta) decreases for
z>1+theta. This includes u=0. It proves (46) with an explicit uniform
margin, not just the strict inequality used for the ray exclusion.

Let p=1+P, x=1+S, t=1+T and let r_i=h_i-1, r_j=h_j-1.
Here p,x,t are positive scalars, not the vector p from Section6. The
palindrome H_m gives the same (46) in the reverse chronological order:

    r_i r_j/(t x)<=q_pair.

Every cut has its own H_level on both sides. Thus r_i<=r p and
r_j<=r t. Combining these three bounds yields

    R_pair<=q_pair,
    p<=q_pair t/R_pair,
    x<=r^2 t/R_pair.                                  (48)

All quantities divided by are positive. The exact total mass is

    M_m=p+x+t+r_i+r_j
           <=(1+r)(p+t)+x.

Substituting (48), and then R_pair<=q_pair, gives

    R_pair M_m/t
       <=(1+r)R_pair+(1+r)q_pair+r^2
       <=D_pair:=2(1+r)q_pair+r^2.

Therefore

    R_pair<=min(q_pair,D_pair t/M_m).                 (49)

This retains both the true preceding mass and the true remaining tail;
neither is absorbed into a new initialization distance.

Write M=M_m, q=q_pair and D=D_pair temporarily. Since M>=8 and
D/q=2(1+r)+1/c<=199/60<4, the crossover t_0=qM/D exceeds1.
The function t^2/(2t-1) is increasing for t>=1 and 1/(2t-1) is
decreasing. Maximizing the bound from (49) on the two sides of t_0
therefore gives the exact elementary envelope

    R_pair^2/(2t-1)
       <=q^2/(2qM/D-1)=qD/(2M-D/q).                  (50)

For the permitted parameters,

    qD<=661/800,
    D/q<=199/60.

Indeed q<1/2, r<=9/20 give
D<=29/20+81/400=661/400, and the displayed formula for D/q gives
its bound using c>=12/5. Consequently, since M>=8,

    [R_pair^2/(2t-1)] / [1/(2M-1)]
       <=(661/800)(2M-1)/(2M-199/60)
       <=5949/6088<1.                                (51)

The ratio in the middle decreases with M because199/60>1; its value
at M=8 is5949/6088. Equations (45),(51) prove that EVERY legal
constant-gradient extension of ANY angle in (26) satisfies

    2f_end<=(5949/6088)/(2S_m+1).                     (52)

This covers all choices of the two checkpoints, all ordinary block
contents in their actual order, both endpoint angles, affine offsets,
positive values, additional dimensions, and arbitrary interpolation
choices for the tail, provided that its gradient is constant at all
actual tail queries and the terminal point. If no such tail exists the
statement is vacuous. No tail feasibility or direction optimization is
silently assumed. At kappa=0 its error is zero as noted above.

In particular, removing only the cap-angle restriction is NOT an escape
from the quantitative obstruction. Sections1--10 give the single joint
conclusion, for m>=128 and the original appended b=d_0:

    max{all quadratic scores, F_ray_n(H_m), V_lens(H_m),
        all Section10 two-block constant-tail scores}
       =K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1),
    limsup of B_n times this maximum <=2/e<1.          (53)

The original all-schedule target remains unresolved. The remaining
geometric change must alter the retained two-block prefix itself or use
varying gradients inside the remaining ruler tail. The full
schedule-dependent complex domain and the other unrestricted mechanisms
identified in Section9 are also still open.

## 11. A quantitative requirement for a genuinely varying tail

Keep ANY angle 0<=kappa<=A in the exact retained prefix (26), but now
permit a varying tail. Denote its physical steps by t_l>=0, its gradients
at those queries by g_l, its terminal gradient by g_end, and put

    T=sum_l t_l, sigma=2T+1,
    Delta_tail=sum_l t_l(g_end-g_l).

This notation changes neither the prescribed steps nor the retained jets.
Its terminal point is y+epsilon d-sum_l t_l g_l. The outgoing
interpolation inequality to the retained jet (y+q,q,||q||^2/2) is

    2f_end<=2epsilon<d,g_end>-sigma||g_end||^2
                                  +2<g_end,Delta_tail>.

Completing the square gives the more useful exact bound

    2f_end<=A kappa epsilon^2/sigma
       +2<g_end,Delta_tail>
       -sigma||g_end-epsilon d/sigma||^2.             (54)

For the H_m geometry, the first term is bounded by gamma K, where
gamma=5949/6088 and K=1/(2S_m+1), by the complete calculation (45)--(51).
Therefore any such witness reaching 2f_end>=1/B_n must satisfy

    2<g_end,Delta_tail>
       >=1/B_n-gamma K
                   +sigma||g_end-epsilon d/sigma||^2. (55)

The true closest-minimizer normalization is still1 by the first retained
checkpoint jet. In particular, (24) implies the quantitative necessary
condition

    liminf B_n * 2<g_end,Delta_tail> >=1-2gamma/e>0     (56)

for any unbounded sequence of successful witnesses of this form.
This requirement keeps every parameter loss. Merely introducing new
gradient directions is insufficient: if
<g_end,g_l>>=||g_end||^2 at every positive tail step, the left side
of (55) is nonpositive. It includes all collinear contracting tails
g_l=c_l g_end with c_l>=1, and all changes g_l=g_end+w_l with
<g_end,w_l>=0. Neither class can escape the obstruction.

When T>0 and g_end!=0, Cauchy--Schwarz additionally gives

    sum_l t_l||g_l-g_end||^2
      >= [ (1/B_n-gamma K)_+ ]^2/[4T||g_end||^2]        (57)

for any successful witness. Equation (55) is stronger because it
retains the sign of the required terminal correlation and the square
loss. No sufficient construction with the required positive correlation
has been established. This is a necessary quantitative interface, not a
new lower bound on W_n and not an assertion of feasibility.

## 12. A different positive-height prefix, and its first failed shortcut

The preceding obstruction fixes the zero height at the second checkpoint.
One can change that geometry while retaining a legal function and exact
normalization. Fix M_0,M_1>=1, A>0, epsilon>0, choose

    0<=kappa<=A, 0<=nu^2<=kappa(A-kappa),
    v= kappa^2+nu^2,
    p=(1,0)/M_0, q=(-kappa,nu)/M_1,
    X=(-A,0), d=-M_1q, y=X+d,
    delta_second=(A kappa-v)/M_1>=0.                  (58)

Use the same actual ordinary blocks and checkpoints of sizes1+A M_0
and1+M_1 epsilon. The one fixed envelope of max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>)
realizes the first two blocks: the first p-plane comparison is unchanged;
in the second block, for 1<=t<=M_1,

    <q,X-tq>=A kappa/M_1-tv/M_1^2>=delta_second>=0,
    <p,X-tq>=(-A+t kappa/M_1)/M_0<=0.

Every query is thus legal. The second checkpoint jet has proximal point
y and value f_j=delta_second+||q||^2/2. The origin is the true closest
minimizer by the same saturated first checkpoint jet, without assuming
anything about the subsequent interpolant.

For ANY legal constant-gradient tail of total mass T, write sigma=2T+1.
The retained second-checkpoint inequality and the optimum inequality,
completed separately as squares, give

    2f_end<=min {
      2delta_second+epsilon^2 v/sigma,
      [A^2-2A(1+epsilon)kappa+(1+epsilon)^2v]/sigma
    }.                                                (59)

These are simultaneous necessary conditions, not an attained formula.
All incoming interpolation inequalities to the old positive-height
queries must still be imposed to produce a witness. They are not removed
by the accepted unqueried-terminal reduction: these are constraints on
the queried tail itself.

The tempting shortcut of treating the first term of (59) as an attainable
retained-height payoff is false. Take epsilon=1, kappa=A/2 and nu=0.
Then delta_second=A^2/(4M_1)>0, but

    X_tail=y+epsilon d=(-A,0)+2(A/2,0)=0.

This is the retained minimizer, so every subsequent gradient and every
terminal error are zero, for EVERY nonnegative tail. The first term
of (59) is A^2/(2M_1)+A^2/(4sigma)>0, whereas the optimum term is zero.
This is an explicit legal fixed-function counterexample to that shortcut,
not merely inconsistent formal data. It also shows why raising the
checkpoint height cannot be evaluated independently of its location.

The screened next tasks are consequently precise: either construct an
actual varying tail satisfying (55) with every old-query inequality, or
solve the joint positive-height prefix/tail constraints behind (58)--(59)
and obtain a value at least1/B_n for all appended b. No such payoff
comparison is claimed here. The present round's completed result is the
quantitative baseline and two-block-mechanism obstruction (53).
