<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001302 -->
# C77. A finite-base uniform ray enclosure for the exact scalar benchmark

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF CANDIDATE, UNVERIFIED. This is not a fact in the
project graph. No verifier is allocated or called. The finite certificate
below was produced by the four explicitly authorized benchmark runs; it is
an exact integer enclosure computation, not a floating-point extrapolation.
Its source, every finite DP row, base-test slacks, and source hashes are public.
The proof still requires independent mathematical/verifier acceptance.

Seventh max3 launch, first round, cumulative round 16. The four-run option is
now CLOSED. Its measured computation, including saved-table certificate
postprocessing, is 32.344760773994494 seconds, below 60; all indices are at most
4095. No seed optimization, installation, or fact_submit was performed.

## 1. Definitions and exact scope

Use the exact recurrences in accepted facts 0be8b54e0a33381c and
b98bb807d514e80d. Set

    P_n = 1+S_circle(n),  B_n = 1+2S_bullet(n),  P_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(a,b) = (a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,
    G(a,d) = 2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,
    P_n = max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(P_k,P_l),
    B_n = max_(k+l=n-1) G(P_k,B_l).

All maxima run over nonnegative integer k,l, with EVERY original split
retained. Write lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), s=sqrt(lambda), and
q_n=sqrt(B_n). In this document q_n is a square-root mass, not an objective
coefficient. To discuss leaf counts, also write U_N=P_(N-1) and V_N=B_(N-1)
for N>=1, and U_0=0. The source paper's W_N equals V_N, not the project's
worst-case functional W_n(h).

The target scalar claim is

    Z_b = lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1),
    Z_15 < (773/100) Z_3.                                  (E5)

The proof below derives existence of these limits as well as their enclosure.
It does not need the source's qualitative objective phase theorem.

The stronger conjecture B_(2n)<=lambda B_n for all n is NOT used. The
argument allows a rigorously bounded positive defect beyond the finite base.
The source's global one-pivot support envelope and the zero-prefix loophole
in C75 are not used.

## 2. Precisely matched literature input

The only non-elementary external input is balanced optimality of the
SYMMETRIC scalar recurrence, as proved in Corollary 4.2 of:

Yu Liu, Kang Chen, Rujun Jiang, Tianyu Wang (2026), *Optimal Recursive
Composition and Dyadic Phase Laws for Gradient Descent with Predetermined
Stepsizes*, arXiv:2609.11788v1, https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11788v1 .

Its complete statement used here is: for U_0=0, U_1=1 and
U_N=max_(1<=m<N) Omega(U_m,U_(N-m)), the sequence is increasing and

    U_(2a)=lambda U_a,
    U_(2a+1)=Omega(U_a,U_(a+1)),  a>=0.                    (2.1)

The second identity includes U_1=Omega(U_0,U_1). The source defines its
kernel K by exactly our Omega. Proposition 2.5 and identical initial values
match its U_N to P_(N-1), by induction. No assertion about equality of ambient
primitive and s-composable certificate classes is imported. In particular,
balanced splitting is NEVER assumed for the asymmetric B recurrence.

The exact paper was downloaded in the prior round; its local text is
downloads/liu_chen_jiang_wang_2609.11788v1.txt. Section 2, Theorem 3.1 and
its proof, Theorem 4.1/Corollary 4.2 and their proofs, and the adjacent-ratio
proof in Appendix C.2 were read. Matlas returned unrelated statements;
this reference is grounded directly in the primary arXiv paper, not falsely
represented as a Matlas hit. No external_refs submission is made because no
fact_submit is authorized. The subsequent scalar estimates are proved here.

## 3. Elementary growth and adjacent-value bounds

First, 1<rho<4/3: lambda>2 and lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)<16. Thus s>3/2
and lambda^(3/2)<4.

For completeness, Omega(x,y)<=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho for x,y>=0.
For positive x,y, put A=x^(1/rho), C=y^(1/rho), and T=(A+C)^rho.
Let D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. For g(z)=log D(exp z), differentiation gives

    g''(z) = rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)
             *((1+t)^rho-1-rho*t)/D(t)^2 > 0,  t=exp z.

The last bracket has value zero at t=0 and strictly positive derivative for
t>0. Hence D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. It follows that
(T-x)(T-y)>=2xy, or T^2-(x+y)T-xy>=0. Since T>=x+y and Omega is the
positive root of that polynomial, T>=Omega(x,y). Zero arguments follow by
continuity. Induction in the full primitive recurrence gives U_N<=N^rho.
Balanced dyadic joins give U_(2^j)=lambda^j; monotonicity then yields

    N^rho/lambda <= U_N <= N^rho,  N>=1.                  (3.1)

Also G(a,d)>=Omega(a,d) for a,d>=0. Indeed, after multiplying their
difference by two, the required inequality is
3a+sqrt(d^2+8ad)>=sqrt(a^2+6ad+d^2); squaring the nonnegative sides
leaves 8a^2+2ad+6a sqrt(d^2+8ad)>=0. Induction gives V_N>=U_N.

Here is the adjacent primitive estimate derived from (2.1). Let
R_N=U_(N+1)/U_N, Delta(r)=sqrt(r^2+6r+1), and

    T0(r)=(1+r+Delta(r))/(2lambda),
    T1(r)=lambda*(Delta(r)-r-1)/2.

Then R_(2N)=T0(R_N), R_(2N+1)=T1(R_N). Both maps fix 1 and have positive
derivative at most 1/2 on [1,lambda]: their derivatives are
(1+(r+3)/Delta(r))/(2lambda) and lambda*((r+3)/Delta(r)-1)/2;
(r+3)/Delta(r) has derivative -8/Delta(r)^3. Starting at R_1=lambda,
binary induction gives

    0<R_N-1 <= (lambda-1)/2^floor(log_2 N) <= 2sqrt(2)/N.

Using (3.1), U_(N+1)-U_N<=2sqrt(2) N^(rho-1). With U_0=0 this implies
U_m-U_(m-1)<=2sqrt(2) m^(rho-1) for every m>=1.

The full dominant recurrence now gives

    0 < V_(N+1)-V_N <= 8sqrt(2) N^(rho-1).                (3.2)

For the upper bound, take a maximizing prefix m for V_(N+1). If m>=2,
compare it to the admissible split (m-1,N+1-m) for V_N. If m=1, use
G(U_0,V_N)=V_N. The derivative of G with respect to its first variable is
2+2d/sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=4. Since m<=N, the bound follows. Strict increase
follows by choosing prefix 1 in the recurrence.

Consequently, with Delta_n=q_(n+1)-q_n and barq_n=(q_n+q_(n+1))/2,

    Delta_n <= 4sqrt(2lambda) (n+1)^(rho/2-1),
    delta_n := Delta_n^2/(4barq_n)
             <= 8lambda^(3/2) (n+1)^(rho/2-2)
             < 32 n^(-4/3),  n>=1.                       (3.3)

The denominator bound used here is q_n>=sqrt(U_(n+1))
>=(n+1)^(rho/2)/sqrt(lambda). No concavity of q_n is asserted.

## 4. Exact merge inequality and contraction along maximizing trees

Let a,b,d>0, write Q=sqrt(d), u=T_b(Q), t=T_a(u), where

    T_a(v)=(v+sqrt(v^2+8a))/2,  T_a(v)^2=G(a,v^2).

If a<=d, then

    G(a,G(b,d)) < G(Omega(a,b),d).                        (4.1)

To prove this, u>Q and Q(u+Q)>2Q^2>=2a. Since t is the positive
solution of t^2-ut=2a, this implies t<u+Q. Set r=t/u and c=a+br.
The polynomial defining Omega gives

    c^2-(a+b)c-ab = ab*(t-u-Q)/u < 0.

Here r(r-1)=2a/u^2 and 2b/u^2=1-Q/u were substituted to obtain the
identity. Thus c<Omega(a,b). Finally,
t^2-Qt=2a+(u-Q)t=2(a+bt/u)=2c<2Omega(a,b), so T_Omega(a,b)(Q)>t.
Squaring proves (4.1).

Consider two successive right-continuation joins in ANY maximizing B tree.
Let their primitive leaf counts be m1,m2 and the remaining leaf count be R.
Their values are a=U_m1, b=U_m2, d=V_R. The same total leaf count permits
merging the two primitive blocks: Omega(a,b)<=U_(m1+m2). Therefore (4.1)
would strictly improve the maximizing tree if a<=d. Necessarily a>d.
By (3.1) and V_R>=U_R,

    m1^rho >= a > d >= R^rho/lambda,
    R < 2m1,
    R < (2/3)(m1+m2+R).                                  (4.2)

Thus the remaining leaf count decreases by a factor less than 2/3 every
TWO edges along a maximizing right spine. This is an optimality argument
for the exact full DP, not a restriction imposed on allowed splits.

## 5. A one-step rounding defect inequality

Extend P_-1=0. Let v(x) be linear interpolation of q_n on nonnegative
integers: v(n+theta)=(1-theta)q_n+theta q_(n+1), 0<=theta<=1. Define

    e_t = max(0, q_t/s - v(t/2)),  t>=0.                  (5.1)

For a maximizing split k+l=t-1, the claim is

    e_t <= e_l                              if t is even,
    e_t <= e_l+delta_floor(t/2)              if t is odd. (5.2)

Only t>4095 will require the odd formula, so delta_0 need not be bounded.

The primitive bounds from (2.1) are

    P_(2a+1)/lambda=P_a,
    P_(2a)/lambda<= (P_(a-1)+P_a)/2.                      (5.3)

For a>=1 the second follows from
Omega(x,y)<=lambda(x+y)/2, obtained using 4xy<=(x+y)^2. For a=0,
P_0/lambda<=1/2=(P_-1+P_0)/2 because lambda>2.

The exact dominant split inequality can be written without a radical:

    2P_i+q_j q_m <= q_m^2,  i+j=m-1.                    (5.4)

It is equivalent to T_(P_i)(q_j)<=q_m. We additionally permit the formal
boundary i=-1,j=m, where (5.4) is equality.

If t=2n and k=2a+1,l=2b, then a+b=n-1. Use (5.4) directly. If
k=2a,l=2b+1, then a+b=n-1; average (5.4) at the two splits
(a-1,b+1) and (a,b), both with parent n. Using (5.3) gives a root at
most q_n in either case before adding the continuation defect e_l.

For t=2n+1, set Qbar=(q_n+q_(n+1))/2. If k=2a+1,l=2b+1,
then a+b=n-1. Average the inequalities with parent/split
(n;a,b) and (n+1;a,b+1). For u=P_a and w=(q_b+q_(b+1))/2, this gives

    2u+Qbar*w-Qbar^2 <= (Delta_n^2-Delta_n*Delta_b)/4
                       <= Delta_n^2/4.

If k=2a,l=2b, then a+b=n. Average parent/split
(n;a-1,b) and (n+1;a,b). With u=(P_(a-1)+P_a)/2 and w=q_b,
the corresponding upper bound is Delta_n^2/4. Boundary a=0 uses the
formal equality in (5.4). In both cases w<=Qbar. The derivative of
y^2-wy-2u for y>=Qbar is at least Qbar. Its positive root is therefore
at most Qbar+Delta_n^2/(4Qbar)=Qbar+delta_n.

Finally, T_a is increasing and 1-Lipschitz in its nonnegative argument:
T_a'(v)=(1+v/sqrt(v^2+8a))/2<=1. Homogeneity and
q_l/s<=v(l/2)+e_l imply

    q_t/s = T_(P_k/lambda)(q_l/s)
           <= T_(P_k/lambda)(v(l/2))+e_l.

Combining this with the preceding parity cases proves (5.2).

## 6. The exact finite base and the resulting uniform error

The saved interval calculation checks the following FINITE certificate:

    e_t=0 for every integer 0<=t<=4095.                  (6.1)

The integer tests and reproducibility information are given in section 8.
Assuming this explicit finite certificate, follow a maximizing right spine
in (5.2) until its index is at most 4095. For visited indices t_i>4095,
put N_i=t_i+1 and n_i=floor(t_i/2)>=2048. By (4.2), whenever i and i+2
are both visited, N_i>(3/2)N_(i+2). Consequently

    n_i >= (N_i-2)/2 > (3/4)N_(i+2)-1
        >= (3/2)n_(i+2)-1/4 > (4/3)n_(i+2).

The last step uses n_(i+2)>=2048. Split the visited nodes into the two
parities of their spine position. Applying (3.3) backward from the last
node of each parity yields

    e_t <= sum_i delta_(n_i)
        < 64*2048^(-4/3)/(1-(3/4)^(4/3))
        < 256*2048^(-4/3)
        < 1/100.                                        (6.2)

For the final exact comparison, 2048^4=17592186044416 exceeds
25600^3=16777216000000. Thus (6.2) holds at every t, with (6.1) handling
the finite base. In particular,

    q_(2n) <= s*(q_n+1/100) for every n>=0.              (6.3)

This is the required uniform estimate. The unproved zero-defect shifted
doubling conjecture has been replaced by an explicit permitted defect.

## 7. Uniform ray enclosure and the oriented comparison

Directly from the original recurrences,

    P_(2n+1)>=lambda P_n,  B_(2n+1)>=lambda B_n.         (7.1)

The primitive bound follows from the balanced join of two copies. For the
dominant bound, B_1=4>=lambda B_0. Induct on n and double both child leaf
counts in any maximizing split of B_n; homogeneity of G and the previous
primitive/dominant lower bounds give (7.1). Therefore the sequence
lambda^(-j)B_(b2^j-1) is increasing for every integer b>=1.

Iterating (6.3) gives

    s^(-r)q_(2^r N)
      <= q_N+(1/100)*sum_(i=0)^(r-1)s^(-i)
      < q_N+3/100,                                     (7.2)

because s>3/2. Monotonicity B_(2^r N-1)<=B_(2^r N) bounds each increasing
ray, proving existence of Z_b without invoking a qualitative phase limit.
Applying (7.2) at N=b2^j yields the uniform enclosure

    lambda^(-j)B_(b2^j-1)
      <= Z_b
      <= lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b2^j))+3/100)^2             (7.3)

for EVERY integer b>=1 and j>=0.

Choose upper ray b=15,j=8 (so the upper finite index is 3840) and lower
ray b=3,j=10 (finite index 3071). These two depths are used for the proved
enclosure; the discovery screen separately compared SAME-j ray ratios.
The finite certificate shows B_3840<90000, hence

    (sqrt(B_3840)+3/100)^2 < B_3840+19.

It also gives the exact strict inequality

    lambda^2*(B_3840+19) < (773/100)*B_3071.             (7.4)

Since 10-8=2, (7.3) and (7.4) establish the proposed E5 inequality,

    Z_15/Z_3 < lambda^2*(B_3840+19)/B_3071 < 773/100.    (7.5)

The computed rational upper quotient in (7.5) is approximately
7.719573399222938; the displayed exact integer slack in section 8 is the
certificate, not this decimal. In fact the same endpoints certify the
slightly stronger comparison

    Z_15/Z_3 < 193/25 = 7.72.                            (7.6)

## 8. Finite computation, integer certificate, and source preservation

Public ledger: research/max3_phase_ratio_discovery_budget.json.

1. Run 1: n<=31, binary64, all original F/G splits, small calibration.
2. Run 2: n<=4095, binary64, all original F/G splits, same-j and adjacent rays.
3. Run 3: n<=63, Decimal with 80 significant digits, independently evaluated
   using the ORIGINAL phi/psi and S_circle/S_bullet transformations.
4. Run 4: n<=4095, EXACT integer interval endpoints on denominator D=2^80,
   all original splits, integer square roots rounded outward.

The source for runs 1--3 is research/c76_phase_dp.py. The source for run 4
is research/c77_interval_dp.py. No NumPy was present and none was installed.
Only the existing Python standard library was used, in one process.

Here is the enclosing arithmetic for run 4. If a,b are endpoint numerators
on denominator D, let

    R_Omega=(a+b)^2+4ab, c_Omega=a+b,
    R_G=b^2+8ab,         c_G=4a+b.

For a lower endpoint, return floor((c+floor(sqrt(R)))/2); for an upper
endpoint, return ceil((c+ceil(sqrt(R)))/2). The result is again an integer
numerator on denominator D. Starting with exact D for P_0 and B_0, apply
these formulas to lower endpoints and upper endpoints separately, and
maximize each over ALL original k=0,...,n-1. Positivity and monotonicity
of Omega,G prove by induction that each stored rational interval encloses
the exact recurrence value. Integer sqrt is checked by its defining
r^2<=R<(r+1)^2 condition. No original split is pruned and no computed
argmax is assumed exact. All possible maximizing splits are retained as
those whose upper bound is at least the largest lower bound.

The complete table is research/c77_dp_run04.csv, SHA256
2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
Its largest interval widths for P and B are below 2.768e-20 and 5.920e-20,
respectively. These widths are diagnostics, not additional hypotheses.

The exact saved-table postprocessor research/c77_postprocess_certificate.py
does NOT evaluate the recurrence again. It encloses q_i by the floor/ceiling
integer square roots of D times the saved B endpoint numerators. With

    D = 1208925819614629174706176,
    s_lower_numerator = 1878397475050142397719609,
    lambda_squared_upper_numerator = 7046136038847924384392699,

it checks, for all fine indices t=0,...,4095, positivity of

    s_lower_numerator*q_lower_(t/2) - D*q_upper_t
                                                    (t even),
    s_lower_numerator*(q_lower_floor(t/2)+q_lower_(floor(t/2)+1))
                          -2D*q_upper_t             (t odd).

The minimum positive slack is
33709536743225496543599832619768805273186776891, at t=4094.
All 4096 slacks, not just this minimum, are retained in
research/c77_finite_base_slacks.csv, SHA256
dfeb3c318e08fba6a889ff78154cf7b762029b86ffa73b64d20fd6f99aa9fb73.
This is the finite certificate (6.1).

For (7.4), the saved endpoint numerators are

    B_3071 lower = 77954524439662110401113475160,
    B_3840 upper = 103225412920462918384305104511.

Writing these as L and H, and the lambda-squared upper numerator as A,
the integer cross-multiplication gives

    773*L*D - 100*A*(H+19D)
      = 98261575859406148591490057041393620527049740462983180 > 0.

For (7.6), replace 773 by 772 in that expression. Its exact positive value is
4020338508518990670593580566018450094572713188395020. The separate saved
addendum research/c77_stronger_threshold.json retains this comparison and
the P_4 upper endpoint, which also proves P_4<77/10. Thus the new sufficient
capacity threshold 193/25 exceeds the finite primitive baseline by more
than 1/50. These deductions use the existing table, with zero recurrence
evaluations; their negligible measured postprocessing is charged in the ledger.

The certificate JSON is research/c77_finite_certificate.json. Every run
and postprocessing source/output hash is in the public ledger. Numerical
floating-point ties were retained in the earlier runs; mathematical ties
are not inferred from them. The independent 80-digit original recurrence
agreed with binary64 to below 4e-16 relative error on its prefix. The exact
interval run independently encloses the full original recurrence and is
the only finite arithmetic used in (6.1) and (7.4).

## 9. A failed shortcut and the remaining original-target obligations

Concavity of q_n is false in the recorded interval computation. At n=721,
the outward square-root intervals on denominator D yield

    q_720 lower = 122144100434199085638661907 / D,
    q_721 upper = 122251834840112040002834251 / D,
    q_722 lower = 122359583204786422642255132 / D.

Consequently q_720+q_722-2q_721 is at least
13958761428275248537/D > 0. The exact underlying B intervals are saved in
research/c77_concavity_countercheck.json. This rejects a proof shortcut;
it does not reject (E5) or the zero-defect shifted-doubling conjecture.
The successful defect argument in sections 5--7 uses only monotonicity.

C75's actual substitution, full-function certificate identities, source
normalization, and index audit remain intact in the public mirror
research/shared_evidence/ca69568f812f2b3d.md. C77 is a SCALAR extension, not
an invocation of that unverified draft as a fact. If both joint candidates
are later accepted, (7.6) provides a sufficient capacity threshold:
an actual length-four primitive seed with A=1+sum h_i>193/25 would use
input horizons 3*2^j-1 and output horizons 15*2^j-5. The bounded output
offset is handled by C75's independently audited phase/adjacent-value step.
No such seed has been found or searched for in this round. The scalar
comparison does not prove the original unrestricted GD target.

For clarity, the unchanged actual seed interface is four nonnegative steps
h_0,...,h_3, s_h=sum h_i, all twenty nonnegative ordered multipliers
Lambda_ij for distinct i,j in {0,...,4}, and the full five-gradient PSD
condition from C75. Explicitly, let e be the all-ones column,
T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i and T_it=0 for t>i,
L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_3,2s_h)^T,
and D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_3(2-h_3),-s_h(s_h+2)). Require

    1+s_h>193/25,
    L_flow^T e=omega,
    D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >= 0.

This is a proposed downstream feasibility target, not a feasible seed or
a new search allocation. C75 retains the full proof connecting it to the
actual all-Q primitive inequality and the actual repeated substitution.

There are no new accepted facts. Verification permission is absent. This
candidate should be audited as one joint scalar proof plus its explicit
finite certificate before any downstream mathematical use.

# Explicit strict interior at a nonbasic five-step word

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is the one
analytical pilot assigned after completed42, under guidance
c3dd5f8ea6c8852a. No fact_submit, numerical evaluator, solver, machine
algebra, or formal call is authorized or performed. The original
exact-profile target remains OPEN.

The construction below specifies the physical word, every one of the30
original ordered multipliers, and the entire six-gradient remainder.
At the concrete parameter t=99/100 it gives the following exact bounds:

    19/2 < A < 48763/5000 < 49/5 < Gamma5_c107,
    1/10000000 <= Lambda_ij < 97       for every i!=j,
    (1/900) P <= M_prim < (7/2) P,
    P = I - ee^T/6,  e=(1,1,1,1,1,1)^T.                 (0)

The upper matrix inequality means strict inequality on e-perp, with
equality on the common kernel. Consequently the full remainder has
kernel exactly span{e}. Its leading five-by-five principal block is
at least I/5400. These are analytical inequalities, not reported
numerical eigenvalues or verifier acceptance.

The word has every step greater than1 and lies within23/1000 in each
physical coordinate of an explicitly justified basic endpoint. It is
itself nonbasic in the precise sense of not being a primitive-join tree
with empty primitive leaves. Thus this is not a small-step anchor.
Its capacity still does not meet the accepted improving-seed threshold.

Accepted inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10-11 (the original
coefficient identity and primitive joins), fact1252b8823bb5a290
(interpolation orientation), and fact4c92d3fc4eb6153e (the threshold).
Draft8ce8b65121586185 is a discovery lead only: all needed baseline
coefficient, flow, and remainder identities are reproduced below in
the same joint proof. No unverified intermediate is cited as a fact.

## 1. Original notation and the concrete word

Normalize smoothness to1. All six formal gradients g_0,...,g_5 remain
independent variables. For a five-step word h, put s=sum h_i, A=1+s,
and x_i=x_0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j. The ordered residual and primitive
potential, in the accepted original normalization, are

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<5)h_i(f_i-f_5)
          +sum_(i<5)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_5||^2.

Every actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0. Set

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,-2h_1,-2h_2,-2h_3,-2h_4,2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<5,-s(s+2)),
    M_prim=D_prim+sym(L_flow T),  sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2.

The accepted coefficient identity is

    L_flow^T e=omega  ==>  V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij
                          =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_5)>.    (1)

Thus the required object is in the original coordinates, with no
rescaling of Lambda, physical steps, or M_prim. The project retains
W=2R. The ordinary benchmark symbols retain their accepted meanings;
in particular C below is a child capacity, not the sequence C_n.

Define exact algebraic numbers

    a=sqrt(2), lambda=1+a, rho=a-1,
    b=(-a+sqrt(10+8a))/2,  p=a+b.

They satisfy a^2=2, rho=1/lambda, and

    b^2+a b=2(1+a),     p b=2lambda.                     (2)

For the joint derivation allow 99/100<=t<1, and set

    delta=1-t, X=t a, Y=t b, C=1+X+Y,
    d=rho C-delta,
    h=(X,Y,1+d,X,Y),
    A=2C+d=lambda C-delta=a+t(lambda p+1).              (3)

The concrete output means precisely t=99/100, delta=1/100 in every
formula below. There are no parameters to be fitted or solved for.

Here are elementary strict bounds used throughout:

    7/5<a<71/50,  8/5<b<161/100,  3<p<303/100,
    397/100<C<403/100,
    1<X<71/50, 1<Y<161/100,
    3/2<d<17/10,   5/2<1+d<27/10,
    39/100<d/C<21/50,
    (29/50)C<C-d<(61/100)C<3.                          (4)

For completeness, F(u)=u^2+a u-2(1+a) is increasing for u>0.
F(8/5)=14/25-(2/5)a<0. Also
F(161/100)=5921/10000-(39/100)a>383/10000>0.
This proves the bounds on b and then p>3 and p<303/100.
The bounds on C,X,Y follow from t>=99/100. Since
rho>2/5, rho<21/50, delta<=1/100 and C>397/100,

    d>(2/5)(397/100)-1/100>3/2,
    d<(21/50)(403/100)<17/10,
    d/C>2/5-1/397>39/100.

The remaining bounds in(4) follow. Every denominator below is positive.

## 2. Joint derivation of the full baseline array

First consider the two-step child (X,Y). Put S=X+Y=C-1 and

    u=2S(1+X)/Y=2(p/b)(1+t a),
    v=2SC/Y=2(p/b)C,
    w0=2-X^2=2delta(1+t),
    w1=(S/Y)[2-Y^2+(2-Y)X]
       =2(p/b)delta(1+lambda t).                       (5)

The full child array, in its three original coordinates, is

             0          1          2
    row0     0          2          0
    row1     2(1+X)     0          u
    row2     0          v          0.

Its divergence is (-2X,-2Y,2S), since v-u=2S. Direct substitution in
the original matrix formula gives all its entries as

    [[w0,-w0,0],[-w0,w0+w1,-w1],[0,-w1,w1]].           (6)

For example M_12=[v(Y-1)-u]/2=-w1 and M_22=-S(S+2)+v=w1;
M_00=w0, M_01=-w0 and M_02=0 follow from the first row of the array.
The middle diagonal is
Y(2-Y)+2(1+X)+u(1-Y)=w0+w1: here uY=2S(1+X) and
w1=u-S^2 show the equality by direct expansion.
To obtain the second equality for w1 in(5), expand its bracket as
2+2t a-t^2 b(a+b)=2[1+t a-lambda t^2]
=2(1-t)(1+lambda t), using(2).

Next define

    c=(C+d)/(C-d),  k=1+2c=(3C+d)/(C-d),
    Delta=d(2C+d)-C^2=-delta(2a C-delta),
    tau=-Delta/(C-d)=delta(2a C-delta)/(C-d),
    eta=2tau.                                        (7)

All c,k,tau,eta are positive. Concatenate two copies of(X,Y) with
bridge step1+d. The bridge gradient is g_2 and terminal gradient g_5.
For this paragraph write P=2,Z=5, beta_3=X,beta_4=Y, and let

    E=sum_(j=3,4)beta_j Q_(j,P)+Q_(Z,P)+d Q_(P,Z)
                                      +sum_(j=3,4)beta_j Q_(j,Z).

The value part of E is

    2sum_j beta_j f_j+(d-C)f_P+(2-C-d)f_Z,

and its gradient part after the actual GD substitutions is

    sum_j beta_j(1-beta_j)||g_j||^2
       +[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_P||^2+(C+d)||g_Z||^2/2.       (8)

Here is the mixed-entry check for(8). In each Q_(j,P), the position
difference is (1+d)g_P+sum_(3<=l<j)beta_l g_l. In Q_(j,Z), it is
-sum_(j<=l<=4)beta_l g_l. For the pair of tail indices3,4 the two
weighted position contributions cancel. Bridge-to-tail terms from
sum beta_j Q_(j,P) equal d sum beta_j<g_j,g_P>, canceled by the
position part of d Q_(P,Z). Tail-to-terminal terms from
sum beta_j Q_(j,Z) equal -sum beta_j<g_j,g_Z>, canceled by Q_(Z,P).
The bridge-to-terminal contributions of Q_(Z,P) and d Q_(P,Z) are
d<g_P,g_Z> and -d<g_P,g_Z>. These exhaust all mixed pairs.
Collecting diagonal terms gives(8).

It now follows coefficient by coefficient that

    V_h=V_left+k V_right-2c E
                         +c Delta(||g_Z||^2-||g_P||^2). (9)

Indeed c(C-d)=C+d matches the value coefficients. For the bridge
diagonal use c Delta=k d^2-C^2; for the terminal diagonal the required
identity is
-(2C+d-1)(2C+d)+k(C-1)C+c(C+d)=c Delta.
Both follow by inserting k=(3C+d)/(C-d). The tail diagonals are
-2c beta_j(1-beta_j), exactly as in(8). Thus no mixed coefficient is
being suppressed in(9).

Add eta to BOTH original coefficients(P,Z) and(Z,P). Set

    U_L=g_0-g_1, V_L=g_1-g_2,
    U_R=g_3-g_4, V_R=g_4-g_5,
    zeta=g_2-g_5, J=X U_R+(X+Y)V_R.

The exact identity

    Q_(P,Z)+Q_(Z,P)
      =-d||zeta||^2-(C+d)<zeta,g_Z>-<zeta,J>

follows from x_P-x_Z=(1+d)g_P+Xg_3+Yg_4. Since
tau=-c Delta/(C+d), adding eta times this identity to the last term
in(9) gives tau[(C-d)||zeta||^2-2<zeta,J>].

Consequently the baseline full array Lambda^0 is

          0          1       2         3          4          5
    row0  0          2       0         0          0          0
    row1  2(1+X)     0       u         0          0          0
    row2  0          v       0         0          0          2cd+eta
    row3  0          0       2cX       0          2k         2cX
    row4  0          0       2cY       2k(1+X)    0          ku+2cY
    row5  0          0       2c+eta    0          kv         0.       (10)

Its exact full remainder is

    M^0[g]=w0||U_L||^2+w1||V_L||^2
          +k(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)
          +tau[(C-d)||zeta||^2-2<zeta,J>].             (11)

This also gives M^0 e=0 directly. For an independent full flow check,
the first two divergences of(10) are -2X,-2Y. The other four are

    v-u+2c(d-C)=-2(1+d),
    4cX-2kX=-2X,
    4cY+2kX+k(u-v)=-2Y,
    k(v-u)+2c(2-C-d)=2(2C+d-1)=2s.                   (12)

Thus every one of the six original flow equations is satisfied.

## 3. A quantitative full baseline margin

Complete the square in(11). Define

    R=X^2/w0+(X+Y)^2/w1=Rbar/delta,
    Rbar=t^2[1/(1+t)+lambda/(1+lambda t)],
    q=tau/(C-d), sigma=k-qR,
    E0=zeta-J/(C-d).

The identity for R follows from a^2=2 and pb=2lambda. Both summands
in Rbar are increasing for t>=0, by differentiating t^2/(1+t) and
lambda t^2/(1+lambda t). Therefore

    Rbar<=1/2+lambda/(1+lambda)=lambda/2.              (13)

For our whole parameter interval sigma>5/2. To see this, (4) gives

    k>(3+39/100)/(1-39/100)=339/61>11/2,
    qR=(2a C-delta)Rbar/(C-d)^2
       <(7/2)C/[(29/50)^2 C^2]
       =8750/(841C)<17500/5887<3,                    (14)

where a lambda=2+a<7/2 and C>7/2 were used. Hence k-qR>5/2.

The exact sum of squares is

    M^0[g]=w0||U_L||^2+w1||V_L||^2
          +sigma(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)
          +q||(X+Y)sqrt(w0/w1)U_R-Xsqrt(w1/w0)V_R||^2
          +tau(C-d)||E0||^2.                         (15)

Expanding the fourth term uses
R(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)-||J||^2
=||(X+Y)sqrt(w0/w1)U_R-Xsqrt(w1/w0)V_R||^2.
Thus(15) is an identity on the entire original space.

We now turn it into an explicit Euclidean margin. From(4)-(5),(14),

    w0>3delta, w1>3delta,
    sigma w0>(15/2)delta, sigma w1>(15/2)delta,
    tau(C-d)=delta(2a C-delta)>11delta.               (16)

Also

    alpha=X/(C-d)<7100/11513<2/3,
    beta=(X+Y)/(C-d)<15150/11513<4/3.

Since zeta=E0+alpha U_R+beta V_R, Cauchy--Schwarz gives

    ||zeta||^2<=3||E0||^2+(4/3)||U_R||^2+(16/3)||V_R||^2.

Define the unweighted tree energy

    E_tree=||U_L||^2+||V_L||^2+||zeta||^2
                                +||V_R||^2+||U_R||^2.

It is at most
||U_L||^2+||V_L||^2+(7/3)||U_R||^2+(19/3)||V_R||^2+3||E0||^2.
Each of these coefficients is dominated by its corresponding
coefficient in(16) after division by delta. Dropping the fourth
nonnegative square in(15) consequently gives

    M^0[g]>=delta E_tree.                            (17)

The tree is the path with vertex order v=(0,1,2,5,4,3). If g is a
scalar vector with sum g_i=0, then

    ||g||^2=(1/6)sum_(i<j)(g_i-g_j)^2.

Bound each difference by Cauchy--Schwarz along its path. The total
coefficients of the five successive squared edge differences are
15,24,27,24,15. For example the middle one is
sum_(i=0,1,2)sum_(j=3,4,5)(j-i)=27; the other four follow from
the same finite sums. Therefore ||g||^2<=(9/2)E_tree, and

    g^T M^0 g >= (2delta/9)||g||^2   for g perpendicular to e. (18)

This argument is also valid coordinate by coordinate for vector
gradients. No equality between two original formal gradients is used.

## 4. Exact tree compensation that fills every missing coefficient

This is the new construction step. Let e_i be the original coordinate
vectors, retain v=(0,1,2,5,4,3), and put

    z=T e=(1,1+X,C,C+1+d,A-Y,A)^T,
    Z=sum_i z_i=2+C+3A-2Y,
    K=6I-ee^T,
    b_l=e_(v_l)-e_(v_(l+1))             for l=0,...,4,
    S_l={v_0,...,v_l},
    F_l=sum_(i in S_l)(Kz)_i,
    w_l^tree=F_l/(z_(v_l)-z_(v_(l+1))),
    gamma_l=1-w_l^tree.                              (19)

The edge differences in their stated order are

    -X, -Y, -(C+d), Y, X.

They are all nonzero, with absolute value greater than1. To eliminate
any implicit linear solve, the five weights are explicitly

    w_0^tree=(Z-6)/X,
    w_1^tree=[2Z-6(2+X)]/Y,
    w_2^tree=[3Z-6(2+X+C)]/(C+d),
    w_3^tree=[6(2+X+C+A)-4Z]/Y,
    w_4^tree=[Z-6(C+1+d)]/X.                         (20)

Define the symmetric matrix

    H=K-sum_(l=0)^4 w_l^tree b_l b_l^T.              (21)

We claim H e=H z=0, as exact identities. The first is immediate
from the displayed matrices. For the second, set F_(-1)=F_5=0.
At vertex v_l, the vector sum_l F_l b_l has entry F_l-F_(l-1)
=(Kz)_(v_l); at the last vertex this remains true because sum_i(Kz)_i=0.
But (b_l b_l^T)z=(z_(v_l)-z_(v_(l+1)))b_l, so(19) proves H z=0.
This is a full coordinate check; it does not infer preservation of
the kernel merely from conservation of total flow.

Choose the exact positive fill size

    epsilon=delta/100000.                            (22)

For unordered tree pairs {v_l,v_(l+1)}, add epsilon gamma_l to BOTH
ordered coefficients. For every other distinct pair, add epsilon.
Equivalently, with all entries in original index order, the final
array is the following complete specification:

         0                     1                    2                    3                    4                    5
 row0    0                     2+eps*gamma0          eps                  eps                  eps                  eps
 row1    2(1+X)+eps*gamma0      0                    u+eps*gamma1         eps                  eps                  eps
 row2    eps                   v+eps*gamma1          0                    eps                  eps                  2cd+eta+eps*gamma2
 row3    eps                   eps                  2cX+eps              0                    2k+eps*gamma4         2cX+eps
 row4    eps                   eps                  2cY+eps              2k(1+X)+eps*gamma4    0                    ku+2cY+eps*gamma3
 row5    eps                   eps                  2c+eta+eps*gamma2     eps                  kv+eps*gamma3         0.           (23)

Here eps is epsilon and gamma0,...,gamma4 mean gamma_0,...,gamma_4
from(19)-(20). In particular the diagonal is exactly zero.

Let L^0=diag(Lambda^0 e)-Lambda^0. The signed symmetric change in(23)
has Laplacian epsilon H, so the resulting ORIGINAL quantities are

    L_flow=L^0+epsilon H,
    M_prim=M^0+epsilon sym(H T).                     (24)

Since H^T e=H e=0, (12) proves L_flow^T e=omega exactly. Moreover

    sym(H T)e=(H z+T^T H e)/2=0.                     (25)

Thus the full common-gradient kernel equation is also exact. Equations
(11),(20)-(24) give the entire M_prim, including every mixed entry.

It would not be valid to add arbitrary positive entries without this
compensation. For example a nonzero symmetric nonnegative addition
with Laplacian N and N z=0 would satisfy
z^T N z=sum_(i<j)Nweight_ij(z_i-z_j)^2=0. Here every z_i is distinct,
so every added weight would have to vanish. The signed compensation
on already positive tree edges is essential. The final coefficients,
as opposed to their increments, are all positive by the next section.

## 5. All signs and the full perturbed spectral margin

First bound every tree weight in(19). Because K is the complete-graph
Laplacian,

    F_l=sum_(i in S_l,j not in S_l)(z_i-z_j).

Every difference has absolute value at most s=A-1<9. There are at
most9 crossing unordered pairs, since |S_l|(6-|S_l|)<=9. Hence

    |F_l|<81,  |w_l^tree|<81,  |gamma_l|<82.          (26)

Every baseline coefficient on a tree pair, in both directions, is
at least2. To check the bridge pair, c>1 and d>3/2 imply2cd+eta>3,
and2c+eta>2. On the two child pairs this follows directly from(5)
and(10). Since epsilon<=1/10000000, each final tree coefficient is

    Lambda_ij >=2-82epsilon >epsilon.               (27)

Every non-tree pair has a nonnegative baseline coefficient and is
increased by epsilon. Thus ALL30 final ordered coefficients obey
Lambda_ij>=epsilon. This includes edges that were positive in only
one direction in the baseline; neither direction is deleted.

For explicit upper ranges, c<71/29<5/2, k<6 and tau<5delta, so
eta<10delta<=1/10. From p/b<303/160 one obtains

    u<10, v<16,
    2cd+eta<10, 2c+eta<6,
    2cX<8, 2cY<9, 2k(1+X)<30,
    ku+2cY<69, kv<96.

Together with(26) these prove, uniformly on the stated t interval,

    epsilon<=Lambda_ij<97 for i!=j,
    |Lambda_ij-Lambda^0_ij|<82epsilon.               (28)

For instance v<16 follows from
2(303/160)(403/100)=244218/16000<16, and
tau<2a delta/(2-a)<(142/29)delta<5delta.
These are proved ranges, not the coordinate boxes of any earlier
numerical search. The original array has not been rescaled to fit
such boxes.

Next control the ENTIRE matrix perturbation. All operator norms below
are Euclidean. K has norm6, each b_l b_l^T has norm2, and(26) gives

    ||H||<6+5*81*2=816.

There are six diagonal entries1 and fifteen strictly lower triangular
entries in T; every physical step is less than3. Consequently

    ||T||<=||T||_F<sqrt(6+15*9)=sqrt141<12,
    ||sym(H T)||<=||H|| ||T||<9792<10000.             (29)

For every g perpendicular to e, (18),(22),(24),(29) give

    g^T M_prim g > (2delta/9-delta/10)||g||^2
                   =(11delta/90)||g||^2
                   >(delta/9)||g||^2.              (30)

Equation(25) and M^0 e=0 then imply positivity on the FULL R^6,
with kernel exactly span{e}. In particular, no omitted off-diagonal
entry, hidden gradient identification, or principal-block shortcut is
needed for(30).

A conservative upper bound also quantifies conditioning in this
coordinate system. In(11), w0<4delta and w1<14delta. The norms of
the four child squared-difference matrices are2, and k<6, giving
an upper norm bound252delta for their sum. The vector defining J
is (0,0,0,X,Y,-X-Y), whose norm is less than4:
X<3/2, Y<2, X+Y<31/10 give its squared norm less than16.
The vector defining zeta has norm sqrt2. Since C-d<3 and tau<5delta,
the norm of the last part of(11) is less than

    tau[2(C-d)+2sqrt2*4]<18tau<90delta.

It follows that ||M^0||<342delta. Adding(29) costs less than delta/10,
so, conservatively, ||M_prim||<350delta. With P=I-ee^T/6 this proves

    (delta/9)P <= M_prim <=350delta P,               (31)

where both inequalities are strict on e-perp. The condition number
on that complement is therefore less than3150. This is a bound for
this matrix in the stated Euclidean coordinates, not a claim about
the condition number of an entire QCQP or a nonlinear solver.

Let B be the leading five-by-five principal block of M_prim. For
y in R^5 and g=(y,0),
||Pg||^2=||y||^2-(sum y_i)^2/6>=||y||^2/6. Hence

    B >= (delta/54)I_5.                             (32)

At t=99/100, equations(22),(28),(31),(32) give exactly the advertised
margins epsilon=1/10000000, full eigenvalue bound1/900 on e-perp,
upper bound7/2 there, and leading-block bound1/5400.

## 6. Capacity, nonbasic status, and exact limits of the result

The concrete capacity is exactly

    A=a+(99/100)[(1+a)(a+b)+1].                      (33)

Using a>7/5, lambda>12/5 and p>3 gives
A>7/5+(99/100)(41/5)=4759/500>19/2. On the other hand

    A<lambda(1+p)<(121/50)(403/100)
                        =48763/5000<49/5,
    Gamma5_c107-49/5=78807/806000>0.                 (34)

Thus the sufficient hypothesis of accepted4c92d3fc4eb6153e is NOT
satisfied. Nothing here asserts an improving seed or a result on
the original asymptotic minimax target.

Here is the promised nearby justified endpoint. At t=1 the word is

    h_*=(a,b,1+rho(1+a+b),a,b),
    A_*=lambda(1+a+b).

It is an ordinary basic primitive construction under the accepted
join law: a=sqrt2 joins two empty words, the next bridge b has excess
b-1 satisfying (b-1)(lambda+b)=lambda by(2), and the final bridge
excess rho C_* solves d(2C_*+d)=C_*^2, with C_*=1+a+b. These checks
justify the endpoint without using any unverified path claim.
At our chosen t,

    ||h-h_*||_infinity
       <=delta max{a,b,1+rho p}<23/1000,
    0<A_*-A=delta(lambda p+1)<41663/500000<17/200.    (35)

In contrast, h itself is not a basic primitive tree. In every nonempty
primitive tree built from empty leaves the first step is at least
sqrt2. This follows by induction: a nonempty left child preserves
its first step; with an empty left child, right capacity C_right>=1,
and bridge excess d_right solving
d_right(1+C_right+d_right)=C_right, substitution of rho gives

    C_right-rho(1+C_right+rho)
       =(1-rho)(C_right-1)>=0.

The left side of the bridge equation is strictly increasing in its
positive unknown, so d_right>=rho. Hence that first step is at least
1+rho=sqrt2. Our first step X=(99/100)sqrt2 is strictly smaller.
This establishes the stated nonbasic status in exactly that class;
it is not a claim about all other possible composition formalisms.

The construction proves two initialization properties: every original
sign constraint has a positive quantitative slack, and the full
remainder has a quantitative spectral margin after removing its
unavoidable common-gradient kernel. The upper bound in(31) supplies
a conservative condition-number bound on that complement. It does
not prove faster numerical convergence, explain why C112 failed,
make an infeasible word feasible, or find a path to an improving
endpoint. Any future numerical usefulness is an initialization
hypothesis that has not been tested in this round.

The bidirected tree is only the set of entries used for compensation.
The FINAL array is dense on every ordered off-diagonal pair; no
support restriction is asserted to be lossless. The final full
remainder is nonzero and has the maximum possible rank5. No reflection
or multiplier-symmetry restriction is imposed on the original cone;
only the added correction is symmetric.

## 7. Retrieval, audit, and resource record

Only one candidate starting construction was used: the explicit
near-endpoint length5 path. No second candidate or unit-word fallback
was needed. The entire baseline and compensation have been derived
jointly above; the new statements remain unverified research evidence.

The fact graph was searched before proof work. Recall covered the
current TASK.md, latest master_guidance c3dd5f8ea6c8852a, the round42
path and final handoff, and the relevant accepted identities. A later
targeted recall found earlier strictly positive ZERO-remainder drafts;
none was used as a premise or mistaken for this rank5 construction.

Targeted Matlas search for prescribed-potential spanning-tree repairs
returned no directly applicable theorem. Web retrieval identified the
tree-defined flow discussion preceding Lemma3 of the primary paper
"A Combinatorial Cut-Toggling Algorithm for Solving Laplacian Linear
Systems", arXiv:2010.16316. Its exact PDF was downloaded. The local
pdftotext command was unavailable (exit127: "pdftotext: command not
found"); the dependent missing-text read also failed (exit2). No PDF
reader module was installed. The exact arXiv HTML was then downloaded,
converted to local text with the standard-library HTMLParser, and
the explicit fundamental-cut/tree-flow definitions were read.
That paper concerns flow repair with fixed resistances; this proof
instead defines signed changes to weights at a fixed potential.
No theorem from it is invoked as a black box. The elementary cut-sum
identity needed here is fully proved in(19)-(21).

Numerical evaluations0; solver calls0; machine-algebra jobs0; DP or
replay0; fact_submit0; formal calls0; installation0; consult0;
native subagents0. File writes, text extraction, and evidence/hash
readback are bookkeeping only. No publication, finalize, revoke,
shutdown, or core/prompt/config edit was performed.

This completes the one assigned analytical pilot. It supplies a
concrete full strict-interior research draft, not formal acceptance,
an improving seed, or completion of the original target. No further
round or resource authorization is inferred.
